- `refinementMultiplier = 0.5` -- refinements count at half weight.

### P0 Override Rule

```
if ANY new P0 found this iteration:
  newFindingsRatio = max(calculated, 0.50)

if total_findings == 0:
  newFindingsRatio = 0.0
```

A new critical finding always signals significant remaining work. The 0.50 floor prevents premature convergence when critical issues are still being discovered.

---

## 6. LEGAL-STOP GATE BUNDLE

> **GATE MODEL, two naming conventions mapped to one authoritative set.** The authoritative producer is `step_emit_blocked_stop` in `deep-review.yaml`, which emits **9 gates** with the `Gate` suffix: `convergenceGate` / `dimensionCoverageGate` / `p0ResolutionGate` / `evidenceDensityGate` / `hotspotSaturationGate` / `claimAdjudicationGate` / `fixCompletenessReplayGate` / `candidateCoverageGate` / `graphlessFallbackGate`. The §1 event shape mirrors this set verbatim and the reducer reads those names verbatim, so treat the event shape as authoritative when writing or reading JSONL state. The §6 table below is the high-level conceptual model and uses descriptive names WITHOUT the `Gate` suffix where one reads more naturally (for example `findingStability` maps to `convergenceGate`); each row carries its event-shape name so the mapping is explicit. `candidateCoverageGate` and `graphlessFallbackGate` are v2-rollout gates that pass trivially when the review-depth-v2 search path is inactive. The gate-model drift cluster (LG-0013, LG-0016, LG-0031, LG-0032) has been reconciled.

Deep review treats STOP as legal only when the full review-specific gate bundle passes together. Convergence math may request STOP, but the workflow must still evaluate these 9 gates and persist a blocked-stop event when any gate fails.

| Gate (§6 conceptual) | Event-shape name (§1) | Rule | Fail Action |
|------|------|------|-------------|
| **findingStability** | `convergenceGate` | Rolling average, MAD noise floor, and novelty ratio must all indicate low-yield review churn | Block STOP, persist `blockedStop` |
| **dimensionCoverage** | `dimensionCoverageGate` | Every configured review dimension must have been examined at least once, with required traceability coverage stabilized | Block STOP, persist `blockedStop` |
| **p0Resolution** | `p0ResolutionGate` | No unresolved P0 findings may remain active at stop time | Block STOP, persist `blockedStop` |
| **evidenceDensity** | `evidenceDensityGate` | Evidence density across active findings must meet the configured threshold | Block STOP, persist `blockedStop` |
| **hotspotSaturation** | `hotspotSaturationGate` | Review hotspots must be revisited enough times to satisfy the saturation heuristic | Block STOP, persist `blockedStop` |
| (no §6 counterpart) | `claimAdjudicationGate` | Each new P0/P1 finding must carry a typed adjudication packet, missing or failing packets veto STOP | Block STOP, persist `blockedStop` |
| **fixCompletenessReplay** | `fixCompletenessReplayGate` | Security-sensitive fix reruns must replay previously closed P0/P1 gates and validate producer/consumer/matrix coverage from the remediation packet | Block STOP, persist `blockedStop` |
| **candidateCoverage** (v2 rollout) | `candidateCoverageGate` | Search debt must be cleared and every required bug class must have candidate coverage before STOP, passes trivially when the v2 search path is inactive | Block STOP, persist `blockedStop` |
| **graphlessFallback** (v2 rollout) | `graphlessFallbackGate` | When the code graph is unavailable, required bug classes must carry cited fallback ledger rows, passes trivially when the graph is available | Block STOP, persist `blockedStop` |

### Gate Evaluation

```
function buildReviewLegalStop(state, config, coverage):
  gateResults = {
    findingStability: {
      pass: rollingStop and madStop and state.latestNoveltyRatio <= config.convergenceThreshold,
      detail: "Rolling average, MAD noise floor, and novelty ratio are all below stop thresholds."
    },
    dimensionCoverage: {
      pass: everyConfiguredDimensionExaminedAtLeastOnce(coverage, config.reviewDimensions) and
            coverage.requiredProtocolsCovered and
            coverage.stabilizationPasses >= 1,
      detail: "All configured review dimensions have been examined, required traceability protocols are covered, and stabilization has aged enough to stop."
    },
    p0Resolution: {
      pass: countActiveFindings(state, ["P0"]) == 0 and state.claimAdjudicationPassed != false,

"missing": ["maintainability"]
    },
    "p0ResolutionGate": { "pass": false, "activeP0": 1 },
    "evidenceDensityGate": { "pass": true, "avgEvidencePerFinding": 1.6 },
    "hotspotSaturationGate": { "pass": true },
    "claimAdjudicationGate": { "pass": true, "activeP0P1": 2 },
    "fixCompletenessReplayGate": { "pass": true, "securitySensitive": false, "requiredRows": 0, "passingRows": 0 },
    "candidateCoverageGate": { "pass": true, "searchDebt": [], "missing": [] },
    "graphlessFallbackGate": { "pass": true, "mode": "graph_available", "missing": [], "unavailabilityReason": "" }
  },
  "graphBlockerDetail": [],
  "recoveryStrategy": "Dispatch the next iteration at the maintainability dimension and re-check after resolving the remaining P0.",
  "timestamp": "2026-03-24T15:02:00Z",
  "sessionId": "rvw-2026-03-24T10-00-00Z",
  "generation": 1
}
```

- `blockedBy`: array of gate names that failed (string[], never structured objects). Empty when STOP is legal, in which case no `blocked_stop` event is emitted.
- `gateResults`: named sub-records keyed by `convergenceGate`, `dimensionCoverageGate`, `p0ResolutionGate`, `evidenceDensityGate`, `hotspotSaturationGate`, `claimAdjudicationGate`, `fixCompletenessReplayGate`, `candidateCoverageGate`, and `graphlessFallbackGate`. This is the full 9-gate set emitted by `step_emit_blocked_stop` in `deep-review.yaml`. Each sub-record has a `pass` boolean plus gate-specific fields (score, covered/missing, activeP0, avgEvidencePerFinding, activeP0P1, securitySensitive, requiredRows, passingRows, searchDebt, mode, unavailabilityReason). The reducer reads these verbatim and does not coerce shapes. `candidateCoverageGate` and `graphlessFallbackGate` are v2-rollout gates, they pass trivially when the review-depth-v2 search path is inactive (no search debt, graph available).
- `graphBlockerDetail`: array of structured graph blockers copied from the latest graph convergence decision. Empty when graph convergence did not veto STOP.
- `recoveryStrategy`: human-readable one-liner describing what the next iteration should do before another stop attempt.
- When the graph convergence verdict is `STOP_BLOCKED`, fold the graph blocker gate name into `blockedBy` and preserve the structured blocker objects under `graphBlockerDetail`.

### Decision Priority

Checks are evaluated in this order (first match wins), as `buildStopDecision` in `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` runs them:

1. **Max iterations** -- hard cap, always checked first. It outranks every veto below.
2. **Consecutive failures** -- three trailing failed iterations stop the run with stop reason `error`.
3. **Stuck detection** -- `stuckThreshold` (2) consecutive no-progress iterations route to stuck recovery.
4. **Convergence mode off** -- `convergenceMode: off` returns `CONTINUE` (`convergenceModeOff`).
5. **Stop policy max-iterations** -- `stopPolicy: max-iterations` returns `CONTINUE` (`stopPolicyMaxIterations`).
6. **Stop candidate** -- all dimensions covered and clean (every configured dimension covered, the four by default, no active P0/P1, coverage stabilized) is a hard candidate; otherwise the 3-signal composite vote with threshold 0.60 is a soft one. Under `--depth-mode=expansive`, a frontier with at least one answered node and none open or active is a third, hard candidate (`frontier_drained`) when neither forms. No candidate returns `CONTINUE` (`noStopCandidate`).
7. **Iteration floor** -- below `minIterations` the candidate returns `CONTINUE` (`minIterationsNotReached`).
8. **Frontier veto** -- under `--depth-mode=expansive`, an open frontier node turns the candidate into `CONTINUE` (`frontierOpen`).
9. **Legal-stop gate bundle** -- review-specific stop gates. If the candidate fails any gate, persist `stopReason=blockedStop` and continue.
10. **Graph decision** -- the candidate is combined with the graph convergence verdict: `STOP_ALLOWED` stops, `STOP_BLOCKED` blocks the stop and `CONTINUE` continues the run.

---

## 2. HARD STOPS

Hard stops are evaluated first and override all other signals.

### Max Iterations Reached

```
if len(iterations) >= config.maxIterations:       // default 7
  return { action: "STOP", stopReason: "maxIterationsReached" }
```

The loop stops unconditionally. Synthesis runs with whatever findings exist.

### All Dimensions Covered with Stabilization

The iteration cap and the halt after three consecutive failed iterations are checked first and can still stop it earlier."

  - "review iteration floor"
  - "antiConvergence minIterations review"
version: 1.0.3.0

# Review minIterations floor

## 1. OVERVIEW

The review convergence check holds a stop below the configured floor. `config.minIterations` when present, else `antiConvergence.minIterations`, sets the floor, capped at `maxIterations`; the shipped default is 2. The floor applies to research and review alike.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Floor Check

`runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` returns `CONTINUE` with the reason `minIterationsNotReached` while the iteration count is below the floor. A stop candidate below the floor never ends the review. The iteration cap and the three-failure halt come earlier in the stop order, so either can still end a run below the floor.

### Stop Order

The review command's `step_check_convergence` places the floor in the fixed stop order after the candidate line and before the gates, so the floor is checked before any gate can end the run.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` | Script | The floor check that returns `CONTINUE` with `minIterationsNotReached`. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | The floor's position in the fixed stop order of `step_check_convergence`. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/assets/deep-review-config.json` | Config | `antiConvergence.minIterations`, default 2. |

### Validation And Tests

|---|---|---|

function stopDecisionConfig(config, loopType, args) {
  const antiConvergence = isPlainObject(config.antiConvergence) ? config.antiConvergence : {};
  const maxIterations = finiteNumberOrNull(config.maxIterations);
  const declaredMinIterations = finiteNumberOrNull(config.minIterations)
    ?? finiteNumberOrNull(antiConvergence.minIterations)
    ?? 0;
  const modeFlagGiven = args.convergenceMode !== undefined || args.convergence_mode !== undefined;
  const configMode = antiConvergence.convergenceMode;
  const depthModeValue = args.depthMode ?? args.depth_mode ?? antiConvergence.depthMode;
  return {
    maxIterations,
    // A floor above the cap could never be reached; cap it at the cap.
    minIterations: maxIterations === null
      ? declaredMinIterations
      : Math.min(declaredMinIterations, maxIterations),
    convergenceThreshold: finiteNumberOrNull(config.convergenceThreshold),
    stopPolicy: config.stopPolicy === 'max-iterations' ? 'max-iterations' : 'convergence',
    stuckThreshold: finiteNumberOrNull(config.stuckThreshold) ?? (loopType === 'review' ? 2 : 3),
    convergenceMode: modeFlagGiven
      ? readConvergenceModeConfig(args).mode
      : typeof configMode === 'string' && configMode !== '' ? configMode : 'default',
    // Only an expansive run carries the key, so a default run echoes the same
    // config bytes it did before the depth mode existed.
    ...(depthModeValue === 'expansive' ? { depthMode: 'expansive' } : {}),
  };
}

// Distinct SOURCE nodes behind the FINDING nodes that answer each question.
// The counts are null when the graph holds no answered QUESTION node, which
// keeps the source-diversity guard unevaluated instead of failed.
function answeredQuestionSourceCounts(nodes, edges) {
  const nodeList = Array.isArray(nodes) ? nodes : [];
  const edgeList = Array.isArray(edges) ? edges : [];
  const kindById = new Map(nodeList.map((node) => [node?.id, node?.kind]));
  const answeringQuestionIds = new Set(
    edgeList.filter((edge) => edge?.relation === 'ANSWERS').map((edge) => edge.targetId),
  );
  const questionIds = nodeList
    .filter((node) => node?.kind === 'QUESTION' && answeringQuestionIds.has(node.id))
    .map((node) => node.id)
    .sort();

              fail("--extend needs a completed run, and this run has status " + config.status + ". Use --lineage-mode=resume to continue an unfinished run.");
            }
            const stateBytes = fs.readFileSync(stateLogPath);
            const rows = stateBytes.toString("utf8").split("\n").filter(Boolean).flatMap((line) => {
              try { return [JSON.parse(line)]; } catch { return []; }
            });
            // One run per iteration number, error and timeout rows included. The integer
            // iteration counts first and a numeric run is the fallback, as the state reader
            // counts them, because a leaf may write its run id string in run.
            const continuedFromRun = new Set(rows
              .filter((row) => row && row.type === "iteration")
              .map((row) => (Number.isInteger(row.iteration) ? row.iteration : Number(row.run)))
              .filter((run) => Number.isInteger(run))).size;
            const addedIterations = Number(extendRaw);
            const priorMaxIterations = Number(config.maxIterations) || 0;
            const priorMaxDurationMinutes = Number(config.maxDurationMinutes) || 0;
            const createdMs = Date.parse(config.createdAt);
            const elapsedMinutesAtExtension = Number.isFinite(createdMs)
              ? Math.max(0, Math.floor((Date.now() - createdMs) / 60000))
              : 0;
            // An unset flag renders as an empty or unexpanded value, never as digits.
            const hasMinutes = /^[1-9][0-9]*$/.test(String(extendMinutesRaw));
            const addedMinutes = hasMinutes ? Number(extendMinutesRaw) : priorMaxDurationMinutes;
            // A run with no duration budget keeps none unless --extend-minutes asks for one.
            const newMaxDurationMinutes = priorMaxDurationMinutes > 0 || hasMinutes
              ? Math.max(priorMaxDurationMinutes, elapsedMinutesAtExtension) + addedMinutes
              : priorMaxDurationMinutes;
            const newMaxIterations = continuedFromRun + addedIterations;
            const sessionId = config.sessionId;
            const generation = Number(config.generation) || 1;
            const event = {
              stem: "deep_review.run_extended",
              scope: { runId: sessionId, sessionId, generation },
              data: {
                priorTailDigest: crypto.createHash("sha256").update(stateBytes).digest("hex"),
                continuedFromRun,
                extension: {
                  addedIterations,
                  priorMaxIterations,
                  newMaxIterations,
                  priorMaxDurationMinutes,
                  newMaxDurationMinutes,
                  elapsedMinutesAtExtension,
                },
              },
            };
            const next = {
              ...config,
              status: "initialized",
              maxIterations: newMaxIterations,
              maxDurationMinutes: newMaxDurationMinutes,
              lineageMode: "completed-continue",
              continuedFromRun,
            };
            fs.writeFileSync(path.join(workDir, "event.json"), JSON.stringify(event));
            fs.writeFileSync(path.join(workDir, "config.json"), JSON.stringify(next, null, 2) + "\n");
            // Config creation writes the stop policy at the top level, beside maxIterations.
            const stopPolicy = config.stopPolicy || "convergence";
            // Review has no question inbox, so under convergence an unchanged dimension set
            // can re-stop on all_dimensions_clean before the new budget is used.
            const warning = stopPolicy === "convergence"
              ? "This run stops on convergence, so it may re-stop on all_dimensions_clean right away. Pass --convergence-mode=off or use stopPolicy max-iterations to keep it running."
              : null;
            console.log(JSON.stringify({ continuedFromRun, newMaxIterations, newMaxDurationMinutes, warning }));

[0m$ [0mcat .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/feature-catalog/deep-loop-mode-classification/depth-mode-flag.md
---
title: "Depth mode flag (--depth-mode)"
description: "The --depth-mode flag on /deep:research and /deep:review selects default or expansive depth, and leaves the registry-driven workflowMode, runtimeLoopType and backendKind classification unchanged."
trigger_phrases:
  - "--depth-mode"
  - "depth mode flag"
  - "expansive depth flag"
  - "deep loop depth mode"
version: 1.0.5.0
---

# Depth mode flag (--depth-mode)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--depth-mode` is a command-level option on `/deep:research` and `/deep:review`. It takes `default` or `expansive`. The flag is not part of `mode-registry.json`, so the hub classifies a request into the same workflow mode with or without it. The behavior the flag turns on lives in the research and review packets. Admission caps the live frontier at half of `maxIterations`, and the seed counts toward that cap, so a defaulted budget is sized from the seed: `min(24, max(loop default, 2 × c × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` for S seeded depth-1 nodes, with c 1 for research and the configured dimension count for review. A run or fan-out lineage with no seeded node keeps its loop default. An explicit `--max-iterations` always wins, even below the derived value.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Hub Classification Unchanged

`mode-registry.json` carries the `workflowMode`, `runtimeLoopType` and `backendKind` discriminator for `research` and `review`, and it has no depth field. Routing resolves the same packet whether the flag is present or not.

### Flag Routing

The command workflow reads `--depth-mode` and binds it as `depth_mode`. The runtime acts only on the value `expansive`. `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` adds `depthMode` to its stop-decision config only when the value is `expansive`, and `runtime/scripts/fanout-run.cjs` passes `depthMode` to fan-out lineages only when the value is `expansive`. A default run carries no depth key in either place.

### Packet Behavior

The expansive behavior is documented in the packet catalogs: [expansive depth mode for research](../../deep-research/feature-catalog/convergence/expansive-depth-mode.md) and [expansive depth mode for review](../../deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md).

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|

README.md:255:Two opt-in flags let a loop reach further. `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a frontier of sub-questions or sub-targets and turns a stop into another pass while one stays open, though never past the iteration cap. `--extend=N` reopens a finished single-executor run in the same session with room for N more passes, and `--extend-minutes=M` adds time. Under the default stop policy it can re-stop at once, so pair it with `--convergence-mode=off` when the run must keep going. A single-executor review can also track custom dimensions beside its four defaults, and a fan-out review lineage always reviews the four.

          dry_run_event_policy: preview-only
          log: "Blocked STOP persisted for review loop. Failed gates: {blocked_by_csv}. Recovery strategy: {recovery_strategy}"
        if_not_blocked:
          proceed: true

      step_handle_convergence:
        action: "Act on convergence decision"
        if_consecutive_failures:
          applies_to: confirm
          when: "stop_decision.reasonDetail == 'consecutiveFailures'. This branch runs instead of if_stop."
          gate_consecutive_failures:
            type: approval_gate
            purpose: "In confirm mode, ask the operator Retry / Skip / Cancel before synthesis starts."

              B) Skip: dispatch the next iteration with the next dimension

          note: "The failure count is rebuilt from the state log, so this gate asks again after each further failed iteration and stops asking once an iteration completes."

The auto three-errors stop (reason error) reaches it through if_existing_terminal_path. The duration stop in step_check_max_duration runs it, once, before its jump to phase_synthesis.             bash: 'node .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/terminal-gates.cjs --config-path "{state_paths.config}" --state-log "{state_paths.state_log}" --registry-path "{state_paths.findings_registry}"'
            note: "The script computes all three gates from the config, state log and registry: evidence by the evidenceDensityGate rule, coverage by the dimensionCoverageGate rule, and scope by matching every open finding's file against reviewScopeFiles.             on_error: "When the script exits non-zero or prints status error, fail closed: bind terminal_gate_results_json to three rows, evidence, scope and coverage, each with status fail, reasonCode gate-script-error and evidenceDigest the sha256 of the script's error text, and bind failed_required_gate_ids to [\"evidence\",\"scope\",\"coverage\"]."

---
title: "DRV-081 -- Review pivot waits for a terminal frontier"
description: "This scenario validates that under the expansive mode the review divergent pivot refuses to run while a frontier node stays open, seeds one revisit direction per closed sub-target the frontier left uncovered and leaves a default run untouched, for `DRV-081`."
version: 1.13.1.0

# DRV-081 -- Review pivot waits for a terminal frontier

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-081`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the review divergent pivot gate for `DRV-081`. It focuses on the refusal while a frontier node is open, on the revisit directions the pivot seeds from sub-targets the frontier closed without covering every dimension, and on the default run, which keeps the pivot exactly as before.

### Why This Matters

The pivot reopens the review from a new angle. If it ran while a sub-target was still open, it would compete with the frontier for the same ground. If it ignored the sub-targets the frontier gave up on, those sub-targets would never get a second look.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the commands below and confirm that the pivot waits for a terminal frontier, seeds a revisit for each closed sub-target it left uncovered, and leaves the default run alone.

- Objective: Confirm that the review pivot refuses to run while a frontier node is open, that it seeds one `frontier-unanswered` direction per closed sub-target the frontier left uncovered, and that a default run seeds no frontier direction even when its registry carries an open node.
- Real user request: Why did the review skip its divergent pivot, and does it still check the sub-targets it gave up on?
- Prompt: `Check that the deep-review divergent pivot refuses to run while a frontier node is open, seeds one revisit direction for each closed sub-target the frontier left uncovered, and leaves a default run untouched. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Run the review pivot cases by file stem, run the workflow case that gates the pivot on a terminal frontier, then read the line in the pivot script that labels a seed `frontier-unanswered`.
- Expected signals: The three pivot cases pass. The refusal case throws for an open node. The seed case creates one direction for the exhausted sub-target and none for the answered one. The default-run case keeps no frontier direction. The workflow gate case passes, and the pivot script labels the seed `frontier-unanswered`.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say the pivot waited until the frontier closed, and each sub-target the frontier gave up on got one revisit direction.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the pivot cases, the gate case and the seed label all pass or print; FAIL if the pivot runs while a node is open, an answered sub-target gets a revisit, or a default run seeds a frontier direction.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that the deep-review divergent pivot refuses to run while a frontier node is open, seeds one revisit direction for each closed sub-target the frontier left uncovered, and leaves a default run untouched. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run divergent-pivot.vitest -t "review divergent pivot under the expansive depth mode" --reporter=verbose`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run stop-decision-yaml-contract.vitest -t "gates the divergent pivot on a terminal review frontier" --reporter=verbose`
3. `rg -n "frontier-unanswered" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/scripts/divergent-review-pivot.ts`

### Expected

Command 1 prints `Tests  3 passed` (the rest of the file skipped). Its three passing cases are `refuses a pivot while an open frontier node remains`, `seeds one frontier-unanswered direction per closed sub-target the frontier left uncovered` and `leaves a default run untouched even when the registry still carries an open frontier node`. Command 2 prints `Tests  1 passed` (the rest of the file skipped), and the case `gates the divergent pivot on a terminal review frontier` passes. Command 3 prints one line, which sets the seed source to `frontier-unanswered`.

### Evidence

- The verbose case lines and summary lines from commands 1 and 2, with each exit status.
- The line from command 3, read from the current pivot script.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: Commands 1 and 2 exit 0 with the expected counts, and command 3 prints the seed source line.
- **Fail**: The pivot runs while a node is open, an answered sub-target gets a revisit, a default run seeds a frontier direction, or command 3 prints nothing.

### Failure Triage

1. The pivot runs while a node is open: check the frontier gate in the review workflow, and count the open and active nodes in the registry it reads.
2. An answered sub-target gets a revisit: the seed must take only sub-targets the frontier closed without covering every dimension, so check the status filter in the pivot script.
3. A default run seeds a frontier direction: check that the frontier seed only runs under the expansive mode, so a default registry with a stray node stays untouched.

---

---
title: "DR-069 -- Expansive seed writes depth-one nodes before iteration one"
description: "This scenario validates that the expansive seed step runs before the fan-out spawn and after config creation, and that a repeated seed does not decompose the topic a second time, for `DR-069`."
version: 1.16.3.0
---

# DR-069 -- Expansive seed writes depth-one nodes before iteration one

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DR-069`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the seed step of the expansive depth mode. The objective is to confirm that the depth-one frontier nodes exist before the first iteration, on both the fan-out path and the single-executor path.

### Why This Matters

A seeded node gives iteration one an open node to work. Without the seed, the first iteration runs with no frontier, and a fan-out run has no nodes to deal across its lineages.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the exact commands below and confirm the order of the seed step in the workflow and the seed behavior in the admission helper.

- Objective: Confirm that the seed step runs before the fan-out spawn and inside `phase_init` after config creation, and that a second seed does not add a second decomposition.
- Real user request: Start an expansive research run and tell me the frontier already holds open nodes before the first iteration.
- Prompt: `Check that expansive depth mode seeds its depth-one frontier nodes before iteration one, on the fan-out path and on the single-executor path, and that a second seed does not decompose the topic again. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Read the step order in the workflow file, run the ordering cases of the YAML contract suite and run the seed cases of the admission suite by file stem.
- Expected signals: The root `step_frontier_seed` line precedes `step_fanout_spawn`. The `phase_init` copy of `step_frontier_seed` follows `step_create_config`. Four YAML contract cases pass, two for research and two for review. Two admission cases pass, one showing that a seeded node keeps root as its parent and one showing that a second seed call adds nothing.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say the first iteration starts from open depth-one nodes, and a repeated launch cannot add a second decomposition.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the step order and all four named cases pass; FAIL if the seed runs after the spawn, after the iteration starts, or adds nodes on a repeated call.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that expansive depth mode seeds its depth-one frontier nodes before iteration one, on the fan-out path and on the single-executor path, and that a second seed does not decompose the topic again. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n "^\s*(step_frontier_seed|step_fanout_spawn|step_create_config):" .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run stop-decision-yaml-contract.vitest -t "seeds" --reporter=verbose`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run frontier-admit.vitest -t "seedNodes" --reporter=verbose`

### Expected

Command 1 prints four lines in file order. The first `step_frontier_seed` line comes before the `step_fanout_spawn` line, and the second `step_frontier_seed` line comes after the `step_create_config` line. Command 2 prints `Tests  4 passed` (the rest of the file skipped), because the filter also matches the two review seed cases. The research cases `seeds the frontier before fan-out spawns` and `seeds the single-executor frontier right after the config exists` pass. Command 3 prints `Tests  2 passed` (the rest of the file skipped), with the cases `seedNodes forces root parents` and `seedNodes seeds a run once and skips a second decomposition` passing.

### Evidence

- The four `rg` lines from command 1, read in file order.
- The two verbose case lines and the summary line from commands 2 and 3, with each exit status.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: The step order matches the expected order and all four named cases pass.
- **Fail**: The seed step sits after the fan-out spawn or after iteration one starts, or a case fails.

### Failure Triage

- The seed step sits after the spawn: check the root workflow order, because a fan-out lineage skips `phase_init` and would start with no nodes.
- The single-executor seed is missing: check the `phase_init` copy, which must follow `step_create_config`.
- A repeated seed adds nodes: check the already-seeded guard in the admission helper.

---

## 4. SOURCE FILES

### Playbook Sources

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `manual-testing-playbook.md` | Root directory page and scenario summary |

### Implementation And Test Anchors

| File | Role |
|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | `step_frontier_seed` at the workflow root and inside `phase_init` |

| `stop-decision-yaml-contract.vitest` | Seed order on the fan-out path and on the single-executor path |

# DRV-074 -- Configured custom dimension joins the review dimension set

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-074`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates how a custom review dimension enters a review run at setup for `DRV-074`. It focuses on three steps: the setup rule normalizes the configured names, the dimension universe lists the four canonical dimensions first and each custom name after them, and the review prompt names every configured dimension in that order.

### Why This Matters

A review that names only the four canonical dimensions cannot check a property such as performance. A custom name that the setup rule rejects, drops, or places in the wrong order would leave the run checking a different set of dimensions than the operator asked for, and the review prompt would not tell the reviewer about the extra dimension.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run this as a setup check. They read the rule that accepts the custom name, the universe that the reducer builds from it and the prompt line that the reviewer receives. No live review iteration is needed.

- Objective: Confirm that a custom dimension named at setup is accepted, normalized, ordered after the canonical four, and listed in the review prompt.
- Real user request: I want the review to check performance as well as correctness. Make sure performance is included and that the reviewer is told about it.
- Prompt: `Start a deep review with review_dimensions: correctness,performance and confirm that performance is accepted as a custom dimension, ordered after the four canonical dimensions, and listed in the review prompt.`
- Expected execution process: Read the review_dimensions input text and the normalize rule in the deep-review workflow, then read the dimension universe builder in the reducer, then run the reducer and prompt-pack cases that name a custom dimension.
- Expected signals: The workflow input names custom names as allowed, and the normalize rule trims, lowercases and drops duplicates, and fails an empty name. The universe builder returns the four canonical names followed by each configured custom name. The prompt-pack case renders the dimension line as `correctness, security, traceability, maintainability, performance`.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator is told that performance is checked after the four canonical dimensions, and that the reviewer receives it in the prompt.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the setup rule accepts `performance`, the universe lists it after the four canonical names, and the reducer and prompt-pack cases pass; FAIL if a custom name is rejected or dropped, if it appears before a canonical name, or if the prompt line omits it.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Start a deep review with review_dimensions: correctness,performance and confirm that performance is accepted as a custom dimension, ordered after the four canonical dimensions, and listed in the review prompt.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n "custom dimension" .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml`
2. `rg -n "function buildDimensionUniverse" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run deep-review-state-reducer.vitest -t "custom dimension"`
4. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run prompt-pack.vitest -t "custom dimension"`

### Expected

Command 1 prints the review_dimensions input line, the risk-order line and the normalize rule among its other custom-dimension lines, each placing custom dimensions after the canonical four. Command 2 prints one line, the `buildDimensionUniverse(config)` definition. Command 3 exits 0 with the cases `tracks and covers a configured custom dimension after the canonical four` and `attributes a finding that names a custom dimension to it, not to the first addressed one` reported as passed, and no failed test. Command 4 exits 0 with `renders a configured custom dimension after the canonical four` reported as passed.

### Evidence

Capture the command 1 matches for the normalize rule, the command 2 definition line, and the names and pass lines that command 3 and command 4 print.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: The setup rule accepts a custom name and normalizes it, the universe builder places it after the four canonical names, and command 3 and command 4 exit 0 with the named cases passing.
- **Fail**: The setup rule rejects or drops the custom name, the universe places it before a canonical name, the prompt line omits it, or a named case fails.

### Failure Triage

If command 1 shows no custom-name wording, the workflow input or normalize rule has drifted, so check the review_dimensions line and the normalize rule in `deep-review.yaml`. If command 3 fails on the universe cases, read `buildDimensionUniverse` and `deriveDimension` in `reduce-state.cjs`, since the canonical names must come first and the configured names follow in order. If command 4 fails, read the `review_dimensions_list` binding in `deep-review.yaml` and the dimension line in `prompt-pack-iteration.md.tmpl`.

### Optional Supplemental Checks

- Canonical-only input: run command 3 and confirm that the case `keeps the canonical keys unchanged for a canonical-subset config` is also reported as passed, since a run that names only the four canonical dimensions must keep the same registry keys.

---

# Custom review dimensions

## 1. OVERVIEW

A review normally audits four canonical dimensions: correctness, security, traceability and maintainability. A setup can name more. A configured name outside the canonical four is a custom dimension. It is tracked and covered like the others, ordered after the canonical four, and judged by its own name. It is not required for severity coverage.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Setup And Ordering

`.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` accepts custom names at setup, normalizes them, and orders them after the canonical four in the risk order. The review registry seeds the four canonical keys plus the custom keys.

### Coverage Tracking

`runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` derives each finding's dimension by matching it against the configured dimension universe, so a custom name that appears in the finding's focus is kept as its own dimension. When nothing matches, the derivation falls back to the iteration's first addressed dimension and then to `correctness`. `buildDimensionCoverage` builds coverage from the iteration records and the strategy dimensions.

### Review Contract And Agent

`deep-review/assets/review-mode-contract.yaml` carries a `customDimensionRule`: priority after the canonical four, configured order, judged by its own name under the shared P0/P1/P2 severity doctrine, and `requiredForSeverityCoverage: false`. The review agent definition allows a configured custom dimension under the same rubric rule.

### Stop Behavior

A covered custom dimension counts toward the all-dimensions-clean stop, so a review with every configured dimension covered and clean can reach that stop.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml` | Command workflow | Accepts, normalizes and orders custom names, and seeds the registry keys. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` | Script | Derives each finding's dimension against the configured universe and builds dimension coverage. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/assets/review-mode-contract.yaml` | Contract | The `customDimensionRule` block. |
| `.skilled/agents/deep-review.md` | Agent contract | Allows a configured custom dimension under the custom rubric rule. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/integration/review-depth-convergence.vitest.ts` | Vitest | The custom dimension stop-path case. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/deep-review-state-reducer.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Dimension coverage with custom names. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/initialization-and-state-setup/custom-dimension-setup-and-universe.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-074: a configured custom dimension joins the review dimension set. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/review-depth-v2-rollout/stop-gate-custom-dimension-coverage.md` | Manual playbook | DRV-075: a covered custom dimension reaches the all-dimensions-clean stop. |

function compareFindings(left, right) {
  const severityOrder = { P0: 0, P1: 1, P2: 2 };
  const severityDiff = (severityOrder[left.severity] ?? 9) - (severityOrder[right.severity] ?? 9);
  if (severityDiff !== 0) {
    return severityDiff;
  }
  return left.findingId.localeCompare(right.findingId);
}

// The canonical four come first in risk order; configured custom names follow in configured order.
function buildDimensionUniverse(config) {
  const universe = [...REQUIRED_DIMENSIONS];
  const raw = config && config.reviewDimensions;
  const configured = Array.isArray(raw) ? raw : (typeof raw === 'string' ? raw.split(',') : []);
  for (const entry of configured) {
    const name = String(entry).trim().toLowerCase();
    if (name && name !== 'all' && !universe.includes(name)) {
      universe.push(name);
    }
  }
  return universe;
}

function deriveDimension(finding, iteration, universe = REQUIRED_DIMENSIONS) {
  const focus = `${iteration.focus} ${finding.title} ${finding.description}`.toLowerCase();
  for (const dimension of universe) {
    if (focus.includes(dimension)) {
      return dimension;
    }
  }
  return iteration.dimensionsAddressed[0] || 'correctness';
}

# DRV-075 -- Covered custom dimension reaches the all-dimensions-clean stop

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-075`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the clean-stop gate for a custom review dimension for `DRV-075`. It focuses on a review run configured with `correctness` and `performance`: once `performance` is covered, the convergence check must stop the run with `all_dimensions_clean`, and the review pivot must not hold a candidate for `performance` open.

### Why This Matters

A custom dimension that the reducer tracks but never marks covered keeps the stop gate open for the whole run. The run then reaches no clean stop, and the pivot seeds an `unswept-dimension` candidate that never closes. The operator sees a review that never finishes even though every configured dimension was checked.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run this as a convergence check on a review loop state that covers the custom dimension. The outcome must come from the coverage the reducer records, not from the default four-dimension path.

- Objective: Confirm that a configured custom dimension, once covered, lets the review stop with `all_dimensions_clean`, and that the pivot seeds no `unswept-dimension` candidate for it.
- Real user request: I added performance to the review. Once a pass has checked it, the review should finish cleanly and not keep waiting on it.
- Prompt: `Run a deep review with review_dimensions: correctness,performance, cover performance on one iteration, and confirm the run stops with all_dimensions_clean rather than holding open on a missing performance dimension.`
- Expected execution process: Run the convergence integration case that records a custom dimension as covered and checks the stop reason, then run the review pivot case for the same covered dimension, then read the line in the convergence script that sets the clean-stop reason.
- Expected signals: The convergence case stops clean with `performance` covered, and its stop decision does not list `performance` as missing. The pivot case accepts the clean-stop trigger and seeds no `unswept-dimension` candidate for `performance`. The convergence script sets the `all_dimensions_clean` reason on the clean-stop candidate.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator is told the review finished because every configured dimension, including performance, was covered.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the convergence case and the pivot case pass and the clean-stop line is present; FAIL if the run holds open on `performance`, stops with a different reason, or the pivot seeds an `unswept-dimension` candidate for `performance`.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Run a deep review with review_dimensions: correctness,performance, cover performance on one iteration, and confirm the run stops with all_dimensions_clean rather than holding open on a missing performance dimension.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run review-depth-convergence.vitest -t "custom dimension"`
2. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run divergent-pivot.vitest -t "custom dimension"`
3. `rg -n "reasonDetail: 'all_dimensions_clean'" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs`

### Expected

Command 1 exits 0 with `stops clean when the reducer records the configured custom dimension as covered` reported as passed and no failed test. Command 2 exits 0 with `accepts the clean-stop trigger and seeds no unswept-dimension for a covered custom dimension` reported as passed and no failed test. Command 3 prints one line, the clean-stop candidate assignment that sets `reasonDetail` to `all_dimensions_clean`.

### Evidence

Capture the pass lines from command 1 and command 2 with their case names, and the command 3 line that sets the clean-stop reason.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: Commands 1 and 2 exit 0 with their named cases passing, and command 3 prints the clean-stop assignment.
- **Fail**: A named case fails, the run stops with a reason other than `all_dimensions_clean` once `performance` is covered, the pivot seeds an `unswept-dimension` for `performance`, or command 3 prints nothing.

### Failure Triage

If command 1 fails, check that the reducer marks the configured custom name as covered in the dimension coverage map, since the stop gate reads that map and the canonical four alone do not satisfy it. If command 2 fails, check the pivot's unswept-dimension candidates for the covered custom name, since a covered dimension must not seed one. If command 3 prints nothing, the clean-stop assignment has moved, so find the new location of `all_dimensions_clean` in `convergence.cjs` before judging the stop.

### Optional Supplemental Checks

- Whole-file stop path: run `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run review-depth-convergence.vitest` without a name filter. It must exit 0 with no failed test, so the canonical stop cases in the same file still pass beside the custom-dimension case.

[0m$ [0msed -n '240,260p' README.md
The commands below are what runs underneath. Type one yourself only when you want a specific workflow or mode.

### Spec Kit Commands

`/speckit:complete` runs the whole path: intake, research, plan, build, verify and save. `/speckit:plan` stops after the plan, so someone can review it before any code is written. `/speckit:implement` builds from an existing plan. Each one accepts `:auto` to run without stopping or `:confirm` to pause at each step.

`/speckit:save` and `/speckit:resume` carry the work across sessions, as [Continuity](#continuity) describes. `/speckit:search` runs the trigger index and the ripgrep recipes over spec docs and skill docs. `/speckit:handoff` hands the work to a fresh session now, instead of waiting for the context limit.

### Deep Research And Deep Review

The deep loops run unattended and stop only when their own evidence says the work is done. `/deep:research` investigates a question one focused pass at a time. Results grow in one research document inside the spec folder. `/deep:review` audits code in passes, ranks every finding P0, P1 or P2 and never edits the code it reviews.

Each pass runs in a fresh context against state kept on disk. A script, not the agent, decides when to stop. A review stays open below its minimum iteration count, while a P0 is active or while a required check still fails, and it ends in a PASS, CONDITIONAL or FAIL verdict. A loop survives crashes and new sessions, because its progress lives in files.

Two opt-in flags let a loop reach further. `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a frontier of sub-questions or sub-targets and turns a stop into another pass while one stays open, though never past the iteration cap. `--extend=N` reopens a finished single-executor run in the same session with room for N more passes, and `--extend-minutes=M` adds time. Under the default stop policy it can re-stop at once, so pair it with `--convergence-mode=off` when the run must keep going. A single-executor review can also track custom dimensions beside its four defaults, and a fan-out review lineage always reviews the four.

A loop can also spread across several AI CLIs at once. Each executor runs its own lineage, and for a review the strictest verdict wins the merge. A pool caps how many lineages run at once. A stall watchdog stops a lineage that stops making progress, and a cost cap bounds what each one spends. The [Deep Loop README](.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/README.md) describes both modes and the shared runtime.

[0m$ [0mrg -n --no-config "frontier|expansive|depth-mode|maxDepth|maxChildren|budget" .skilled/agents/deep-review.md | head -50
128:4. EXECUTE REVIEW ───► 3-5 focused analysis actions within budget
179:- Contradictory evidence: cite both sides, search counterevidence once within budget, then adjudicate or record unresolved contradiction.
265:**Frontier proposals (expansive depth runs only).** When the iteration prompt carries a FRONTIER block, you may append `frontier_proposal` rows to the delta, at most the number that block allows, one per sub-target the evidence opened inside the node you were reviewing:
266:`{"type":"frontier_proposal","parentId":"<the node id the prompt named>","question":"<what to review, not a restatement of an existing node>","scope":"<paths inside the review target>","evidence":"<file:line that opened it>","budget":<optional positive integer, never above the parent's>}`
267:You only propose. The workflow's admission step decides, and it checks relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle in that order, so it prunes a row that lacks a question, evidence or scope, names a parent the store does not hold, sits outside a `files` review target, repeats a known node, would exceed the depth, fan-out or budget caps, or would close a parent cycle. Never write `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, `searchDebt` or any coverage surface, and never emit a `frontier_proposal` row when the prompt has no FRONTIER block. When the block names a node, add `"frontierNodeId"` to the iteration record as the block says.

---

## 4. STATE FILES

Review mode stores its packet under the resolved local review path rooted at `{spec_folder}/review/`:

| File | Location | Format | Purpose |
|------|----------|--------|---------|
| Config | `review/deep-review-config.json` | JSON | Review parameters (immutable) |
| State | `review/deep-review-state.jsonl` | JSONL | Iteration log (append-only) |
| Registry | `review/deep-review-findings-registry.json` | JSON | Reducer-owned finding registry |
| Strategy | `review/deep-review-strategy.md` | Markdown | Dimensions, findings, next focus |
| Dashboard | `review/deep-review-dashboard.md` | Markdown | Auto-generated review dashboard |
| Iterations | `review/iterations/iteration-NNN.md` | Markdown | Per-iteration findings (write-once) |
| Report | `review/review-report.md` | Markdown | Final 9-section review report |
| Pause | `review/.deep-review-pause` | Sentinel | Pause between iterations |

### Lifecycle Modes

| Mode | Effect |
|------|--------|
| `resume` | Continue the current review lineage without resetting generation |
| `restart` | Archive current review state and start a new generation |
| `fork` (deferred) | Reserved. Start a sibling lineage branch with explicit parent linkage. Not runtime-wired. |
| `completed-continue` | `--extend=N`: snapshot the completed review report as `review-report-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md` and reopen the run for N more iterations in the same session |

---

## 5. REVIEW DIMENSIONS

| ID | Dimension | Priority | Description |
|----|-----------|----------|-------------|
| D1 | Correctness | 1 | Logic errors, off-by-one, wrong return types, broken invariants |
| D2 | Security | 2 | Injection, auth bypass, secrets exposure, unsafe deserialization |
| D3 | Traceability | 3 | Spec/code alignment, checklist evidence, cross-reference integrity |
| D4 | Maintainability | 4 | Patterns, clarity, documentation quality, ease of safe follow-on changes |

---
---

## 8. REVIEW CONVERGENCE

| Signal | Weight | Description |
|--------|--------|-------------|
| Rolling Average | 0.30 | Last 2 severity-weighted `newFindingsRatio` values average below `0.08` |
| MAD Noise Floor | 0.25 | Latest ratio within noise floor |
| Dimension Coverage | 0.45 | Every configured dimension (the four by default) plus required traceability protocols covered, with `minStabilizationPasses >= 1` |

**Key defaults:** `maxIterations=7`, `convergenceThreshold=0.10`, `rollingStopThreshold=0.08`, `noProgressThreshold=0.05`, `stuckThreshold=2`, `minIterations=2` (review floor, `antiConvergence.minIterations`), `minStabilizationPasses=1`

**P0 override:** Any new P0 finding sets `newFindingsRatio >= 0.50`, blocking convergence.

**Frontier veto:** Under `--depth-mode=expansive` an open or active frontier node turns a stop candidate into `CONTINUE` with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. It never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the review floor. A drained frontier (at least one answered node, none open or active) forms the hard candidate `frontier_drained` when no other candidate does; every legal-stop gate still applies.

---

## 9. AGENT ITERATION CHECKLIST

Each @deep-review iteration:
1. Read `deep-review-state.jsonl`, `deep-review-findings-registry.json`, and `deep-review-strategy.md`
2. Determine focus dimension from strategy "Next Focus"
3. Write `review/iterations/iteration-NNN.md` with P0/P1/P2 findings
4. Run adversarial self-check on any P0 findings (Hunter/Skeptic/Referee)
5. Update `deep-review-strategy.md` (findings, coverage, next focus)

|--------|---------|
| `complete` | Normal iteration with findings |
| `timeout` | Exceeded time/tool budget |
| `error` | Failed to produce outputs |
| `stuck` | No new information found |
| `insight` | Low ratio but conceptual breakthrough |
| `thought` | Analytical-only, no evidence |

---

## 7. CONVERGENCE DECISION TREE

Quality guards (source diversity, focus alignment, no single-weak-source) must pass before STOP.

```text
Max iterations reached?
  Yes --> STOP

3 consecutive failed iterations?
  Yes --> STOP (error)

stuckThreshold consecutive no-progress?
  Yes --> STUCK_RECOVERY
    Recovery works? --> CONTINUE
    Recovery fails? --> STOP (with gaps)

All questions answered, or composite convergence (3-signal weighted > 0.60)?
  Yes --> STOP candidate
    Below minIterations? --> CONTINUE
    Expansive run with an open frontier node? --> CONTINUE
    Otherwise --> STOP (converged), once the quality guards pass

Otherwise --> CONTINUE
```

### 3-Signal Convergence Model

| Signal | Weight | Min Iterations | Votes STOP When |
|--------|--------|---------------|-----------------|
| Rolling Average | 0.30 | 3 | avg(last 3 newInfoRatios) < convergenceThreshold |
| MAD Noise Floor | 0.35 | 4 | latest ratio <= MAD * 1.4826 |
| Question Entropy | 0.35 | 1 | answered / total questions >= 0.85 |

**Composite stop threshold:** 0.60 -- weighted stop score must exceed this before quality guards are evaluated.

---

## 8. AGENT ITERATION CHECKLIST

Each @deep-research iteration:
1. Read `deep-research-state.jsonl` and `deep-research-strategy.md`
2. Determine focus from reducer-owned strategy "Next Focus"
3. Execute 3-5 research actions (WebFetch, Grep, Read)
4. Write `research/iterations/iteration-NNN.md` with findings
5. Record the iteration through the append gateway, the only way a record reaches `deep-research-state.jsonl`
6. Let the workflow reducer update `deep-research-strategy.md`, `findings-registry.json`, and `deep-research-dashboard.md`

// and blocked nodes stay frontier-only: their question is closed, and filing them
// as debt would report work the run is no longer allowed to do.
function mergeFrontierIntoSearchLedger(searchLedgerState, frontier) {
  const candidateCoverage = searchLedgerState.candidateCoverage;
  const debtById = new Map(
    (Array.isArray(searchLedgerState.searchDebt) ? searchLedgerState.searchDebt : [])
      .map((entry) => [entry.id, entry]),
  );
  for (const node of frontier.nodes) {
    const bugClass = `frontier:${node.nodeId}`;
    const iteration = node.iteration ?? 0;
    if (node.status === 'open' || node.status === 'active') {
      const id = `frontier-${node.nodeId}`;
      debtById.set(id, {
        id,
        iteration,
        targetRefs: frontierScopeRefs(node.scope),
        bugClass,
        disposition: 'deferred',
        rationale: `Frontier node ${node.nodeId} is still open: ${node.question}`,
        evidenceRefs: [`frontier-nodes.jsonl#${node.nodeId}`],
        reason: 'frontier node open',
        frontier: true,
      });
      addCandidateClass(candidateCoverage, bugClass, 'deferred', iteration);
    } else if (node.status === 'answered') {
      addCandidateClass(candidateCoverage, bugClass, 'covered', iteration);
    } else if (node.status === 'pruned' || node.status === 'abstained') {
      addCandidateClass(candidateCoverage, bugClass, 'ruled_out', iteration);
    }
  }
  searchLedgerState.searchDebt = [...debtById.values()]
    .sort((left, right) => left.iteration - right.iteration || left.id.localeCompare(right.id));
  for (const key of ['covered', 'ruledOut', 'deferred', 'blocked']) {
    candidateCoverage[key].sort((left, right) => left.localeCompare(right));
  }
  for (const state of Object.values(candidateCoverage.byBugClass)) {
    state.iterations.sort((left, right) => Number(left) - Number(right));
  }
  return searchLedgerState;
}

/**
 * Enforce record validation at the field level. parseJsonlDetailed only guards
 * JSON syntax; these warnings surface schema drift without changing the
 * reducer's default pass/fail behavior for currently-valid state.
 */
function validateReviewRecordFields(records) {
  const warnings = [];
  const severityBuckets = ['P0', 'P1', 'P2'];
  (Array.isArray(records) ? records : []).forEach((record, index) => {
    if (!record || typeof record !== 'object') {
      return;
    }
    if (typeof record.type !== 'string' || !record.type) {
      warnings.push({ index, rule: 'type-required', detail: 'record is missing a string `type` field' });
      return;
    }
    if (record.type === 'iteration' || record.event === 'synthesis_complete') {
      if (record.mode !== undefined && record.mode !== 'review') {
        warnings.push({ index, rule: 'mode-review', detail: `mode must be "review", saw "${record.mode}"` });
      }
    }
    if (record.type === 'iteration') {
      if (record.newFindingsRatio !== undefined
        && (typeof record.newFindingsRatio !== 'number'
          || record.newFindingsRatio < 0 || record.newFindingsRatio > 1)) {
        warnings.push({ index, rule: 'newFindingsRatio-range', detail: 'newFindingsRatio must be 0.0-1.0' });
      }
      if (record.findingsSummary !== undefined) {
        const value = record.findingsSummary;
        if (!value || typeof value !== 'object' || Array.isArray(value)
          || !severityBuckets.every((key) => key in value)) {
          warnings.push({ index, rule: 'findingsSummary-severity-keys', detail: 'findingsSummary must contain P0, P1, P2 keys' });
        }
      }
      // findingsNew is contractually polymorphic: prompt_pack_iteration.md.tmpl documents it
      // as an array of new-finding entries, while legacy records emit a {P0,P1,P2} count
      // object (state_jsonl.md). Both shapes are genuinely valid -- mirror the same
      // tolerance post-dispatch-validate.ts already applies via hasPositiveSeverityCount().
      if (record.findingsNew !== undefined) {
        const value = record.findingsNew;
        const isValidArray = Array.isArray(value);
        const isValidSeverityObject = !isValidArray && value && typeof value === 'object'
          && severityBuckets.every((key) => key in value);
        if (!isValidArray && !isValidSeverityObject) {
          warnings.push({ index, rule: 'findingsNew-shape', detail: 'findingsNew must be an array of findings or an object with P0, P1, P2 keys' });
        }
      }
      if (record.findingDetails !== undefined && !Array.isArray(record.findingDetails)) {
        warnings.push({ index, rule: 'findingDetails-array', detail: 'findingDetails must be an array' });
      }
    }
  });
  return warnings;
}

    `- Failed pivots: ${registry.divergence.failed.length}`,
// The frontier metrics only exist once the expansive depth mode projects a
// frontier, so a default run renders no section at all rather than an empty one.
function renderFrontierSection(frontier) {
  if (!frontier || typeof frontier !== 'object') return [];
  const stats = frontier.stats || {};
  const nodes = Array.isArray(frontier.nodes) ? frontier.nodes : [];
  // A visit that covered no new dimension is depth spent again on ground the
  // run had already walked, which the size and status counts cannot show.
  const revisits = nodes.reduce((total, node) => {
    const visits = Number(node?.visits) || 0;
    const covered = Array.isArray(node?.coveredDimensions) ? node.coveredDimensions.length : 0;
    return total + Math.max(0, visits - covered);
  }, 0);
  const budgetEntries = Object.entries(
    frontier.budget && typeof frontier.budget === 'object' ? frontier.budget : {},
  );
  return [
    '<!-- ANCHOR:frontier -->',
    '## 10B. FRONTIER',
    `- Frontier size: ${stats.total} nodes (${stats.open} open, ${stats.active} active, ${stats.answered} answered, ${stats.exhausted} exhausted, ${stats.blocked} blocked)`,
    `- Pruned nodes: ${stats.pruned}`,
    `- Revisits: ${revisits}`,
    ...(budgetEntries.length
      ? budgetEntries.map(([band, entry]) => `- Budget ${band}: ${entry.spent} of ${entry.allotted} iterations spent`)
      : ['- Budget: not recorded']),
    ...(frontier.activeNodeId
      ? [`- Active node: ${frontier.activeNodeId} (${frontier.activeNextDimension})`]
      : ['- Active node: none']),
    '',
    '<!-- /ANCHOR:frontier -->',
  ];
}

[0m$ [0msed -n '55,90p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/convergence/convergence.md; sed -n '125,140p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/convergence/convergence.md
### Convergence Modes

The shared convergence runtime accepts four values. The workflow keeps hard terminal boundaries outside mode-specific convergence handling.

| Mode | Live Behavior |
|------|---------------|
| `default` | Uses the normal full-history convergence signals and sends a legal STOP to synthesis. This is the fallback when no mode is supplied. |
| `off` | Skips convergence-driven STOP candidates. Max iterations, pause or cancellation, manual stop, recovery halt, and unrecoverable error behavior remain active. |
| `sliding-window` | Uses a bounded graph-novelty window, defaulting to 5 snapshots, while preserving the ordinary legal-stop and synthesis path. |
| `divergent` | Computes convergence normally, but translates an eligible legal STOP into a three-seat direction-selection pivot and continues from the selected focus. |

#### Expansive Depth Mode

`--depth-mode=expansive` grows a frontier of follow-up questions beside the questions seeded at init. The value lands in `antiConvergence.depthMode`, and the caps live in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, `budgetSplit` 0.5/0.35/0.15 for depths 1, 2 and 3, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. The run cap never prunes a depth-1 seed, which only the root-children cap bounds, but every seed that is not pruned, answered ones included, counts toward the live total that later admissions are checked against. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(10, 2 × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes, 16 at the full six seeds, and the seed step prints the derived budget. The node cap stays half that budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. A fan-out parent seeds before any config exists, so its cap falls back to 10. An absent flag means `default`, which keeps the default behavior; `research-depth-mode-golden.vitest` proves it after stripping the expansive-only persona paragraph from the CLI lineage prompt, the one byte change.

After the `minIterations` floor, an expansive run with open or active frontier nodes turns a soft stop candidate into CONTINUE with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. The veto never creates a stop of its own and never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. Under `--convergence-mode=divergent` the pivot becomes eligible only once no frontier node is open or active.

Tested in `stop-decision.vitest`.

#### Divergent Pivot Contract

Research pivots are eligible only when the workflow decision is `STOP` and the exact internal reason is `composite_converged` or `all_questions_answered`. The legal-stop and quality gates run before this branch. `maxIterationsReached`, `blockedStop`, `stuckRecovery`, `minIterationsNotReached`, `error`, `manualStop`, and `userPaused` are explicitly excluded. Max iterations, pause or cancellation, manual stop, and unrecoverable error therefore never dispatch a pivot Council.

Each eligible pivot runs one native, in-process Council round with exactly three distinct seats: analytical, critical, and pragmatic.
If that round misses quorum, because a seat fails to return or returns a report that does not parse, the pivot reopens once as attempt 2 with three fresh seat outputs. A second quorum miss, or three valid seats that do not agree, fails the pivot closed. `maxCouncilSeatOutputs` caps one council attempt at 3 seat outputs, so a pivot records at most 6.

- It does not use an external CLI.
- The transaction requires all three seat returns to be parse-valid and at least two seats to materially endorse the same candidate without a high-severity blocker.
- Research candidates come from persisted adjacent questions, contradiction or verification gaps, missing source classes, alternate evidence methods, and independent checks of recent findings.
- A completed pivot restores the selected candidate's focus and returns to the research loop; a failed pivot fails closed to synthesis.

Every pivot dispatches three seats, or six when its first attempt misses quorum, so a run that pivots has proportionally higher model cost and duration than the same run under `default`, `off`, or `sliding-window`. Pivot evidence is isolated under `<artifactRoot>/divergent/pivots/<pivotId>/council/**`, including config, append-only state, three seat artifacts, deliberation, and report, plus an attempt-2 folder with its own seat artifacts and deliberation after a retry. This path is distinct from ordinary planning Council artifacts under a spec packet's `ai-council/**` tree.

---

## 2. STOP CONTRACT
```text
1. If iteration count >= maxIterations, STOP with maxIterationsReached.
2. After 3 consecutive failed iterations, STOP with stop reason error.
3. If stuckCount >= stuckThreshold, enter stuck recovery.
4. If convergenceMode is off or stopPolicy is max-iterations, CONTINUE.
5. If all key questions have evidence-backed answers, nominate STOP. Otherwise compute composite convergence signals and nominate STOP when the weighted stop score > 0.60. With no candidate, CONTINUE.
6. If iteration count < minIterations, CONTINUE with minIterationsNotReached.
7. If depthMode is expansive and a frontier node is open or active, CONTINUE with frontierOpen.
8. Evaluate the quality guards for every STOP candidate except hard max-iteration stop. A failed guard returns CONTINUE with qualityGuardFailed and emits no blocked_stop event.
9. Block every non-terminal STOP until a graph decision exists. A graph STOP_BLOCKED emits blockedStop and continues with recovery focus.
10. If the guards pass and the graph allows the stop, pivot for an eligible divergent reason or STOP normally.
```

### Composite Signal Weights

| Signal | Weight | Min Iterations | Measures |

Author: Michel Kerkmeester <redacted>
Date:   Wed Nov 18 10:05:34 2026 +0100

    docs(system-deep-loop): document the loop default for an empty seed share
    
    The review docs said each fan-out lineage sizes its budget from its share
    of the seed, which left out a lineage dealt no seed node. That lineage,
    and a single run whose seeds were all pruned, now runs at its loop
    default, 12 iterations native and 7 CLI. The review command row, skill,
    README, catalog, convergence and loop-protocol references, the flag
    playbook page and the shared depth-mode flag entry say so.
    
    Commit-Id: 0014907

-| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths, and a run-wide `maxFrontierNodes` cap that defaults to half of `--max-iterations`), keeps node rows in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl` (the reducer derives answered and budget-exhausted status from them on every reduce), holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open, and, once every node is closed and at least one was answered, forms a `frontier_drained` stop candidate that the legal-stop gates still decide, so the run can stop before its cap. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name, and the depth-1 band gets only `budgetSplit.depth1` (0.5) of `--max-iterations`, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` to fund the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, the minIterations floor, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review; split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |

An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. The run-wide `maxFrontierNodes` cap defaults to half of `maxIterations`, and every seed that is not pruned counts toward it.  # Depth mode flag (--depth-mode)
 
 ## 1. OVERVIEW
 
-`--depth-mode` is a command-level option on `/deep:research` and `/deep:review`. It takes `default` or `expansive`. The flag is not part of `mode-registry.json`, so the hub classifies a request into the same workflow mode with or without it. The behavior the flag turns on lives in the research and review packets. Admission caps the live frontier at half of `maxIterations`, and the seed counts toward that cap, so a defaulted budget is sized from the seed: `min(24, max(loop default, 2 × c × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` for S seeded depth-1 nodes, with c 1 for research and the configured dimension count for review. An explicit `--max-iterations` always wins, even below the derived value.
+`--depth-mode` is a command-level option on `/deep:research` and `/deep:review`. It takes `default` or `expansive`. The flag is not part of `mode-registry.json`, so the hub classifies a request into the same workflow mode with or without it. The behavior the flag turns on lives in the research and review packets. Admission caps the live frontier at half of `maxIterations`, and the seed counts toward that cap, so a defaulted budget is sized from the seed: `min(24, max(loop default, 2 × c × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` for S seeded depth-1 nodes, with c 1 for research and the configured dimension count for review. A run or fan-out lineage with no seeded node keeps its loop default. An explicit `--max-iterations` always wins, even below the derived value.

 # Expansive depth mode (--depth-mode=expansive)
 
 ## 1. OVERVIEW
 
-`--depth-mode` takes `default` or `expansive`. A run without the flag is unchanged. `expansive` turns on a research frontier: the run seeds depth-one nodes from the topic before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows as nodes under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps, and holds a convergence stop while frontier nodes stay open. Two live research pilots ran on one topic, and both failed the criterion that a stop candidate forms before the iteration cap: each run stopped on `maxIterationsReached`. This page describes the shipped code and its tests.
+`--depth-mode` takes `default` or `expansive`.
