### Expansive Depth Mode

- `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a depth-capped frontier of follow-up questions beside the seeded key questions. An absent flag means `default`, which keeps the default behavior. The one byte change is the expansive-only persona paragraph in the CLI lineage prompt, which the golden test strips before it compares.
- `step_frontier_seed` writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1 through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed`, after `step_create_config`, or before `step_fanout_spawn` on a fan-out run.
- The caps live in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. The depth-1 seed answers only to the root-children cap, but every seed that is not pruned counts toward the run cap. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(10, 2 × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes, 16 at the full six seeds, and the seed step prints the derived budget. The node cap stays half that budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1.
- `step_frontier_admit` runs before `step_reduce_state` and prunes a `frontier_proposal` on the first failed check among relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle. Admitted nodes land in `research/inbox.jsonl` with `origin: "frontier"`.
- Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first, then the least visited, then the oldest, and writes `Frontier node <id> (depth N, scope S): <question>`.
- After the `minIterations` floor an open or active node turns a soft stop into CONTINUE with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. It never creates a stop and never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`.
- A fan-out run deals the seeded depth-1 nodes round-robin across lineages and merges them back under a partition guard, so no dealt node goes unreported.

### Invocation Contract

This skill is invoked exclusively through `/deep:research:auto` or `/deep:research:confirm` -- the command YAML owns state, dispatch, convergence, and synthesis. Never simulate the loop with ad hoc shell dispatch, nested CLI loops, direct `@deep-research` Task dispatch, `/tmp` prompt files, or state outside the resolved local research packet.


The stuck count and the consecutive failure count are rebuilt from the state log on every pass, so a resumed run counts the same as one that never paused. Failed iterations stay out of the ratio signals. Three trailing failed iterations stop the loop with stop reason `error`. In confirm mode only, the workflow first asks the operator Retry, Skip or Cancel. Auto mode has no such question.

Each pass also compares `maxDurationMinutes` with the config `createdAt`. When the limit is reached the loop skips the dispatch and goes to synthesis with stop reason `maxDurationReached`.

### Expansive Depth Mode

- `--depth-mode=expansive` keeps a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. An absent flag means `default`, which runs exactly as before.
- The flag writes `antiConvergence.depthMode` and the caps in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6 (the review root seed takes up to 8 depth-1 sub-targets, against 6 for research), a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. The depth-1 seed answers only to the root-children cap, but every seed that is not pruned counts toward the run cap. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review; split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap. `antiConvergence.depthMode` is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` caps are in neither its tunable nor its locked list.
- `step_frontier_seed` writes the depth-1 nodes before iteration 1 through `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs seed --target review`, after `step_create_config` or before `step_fanout_spawn` on a fan-out run. A merge with no diff of its own is pruned `merge-no-own-diff` and an index rebuild `generated-artifact`.
- `step_frontier_admit` runs before the reducer and prunes a `frontier_proposal` on the first failed check among relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, written only by `frontier-admit.cjs`; review has no inbox.
- `step_read_state` reads the active node and sets `next_dimension` to its next uncovered dimension, `step_frontier_activate` marks it active before dispatch, and the prompt pack's `{frontier_block}` names the node. Next Focus takes the blocked stop, then the pivot, then the node, then the leaf focus, then the first uncovered dimension.
- While a node stays open the frontier veto turns a stop candidate into `CONTINUE` with reasonDetail `frontierOpen`. It never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the review floor, and stuck recovery closes the active node `exhausted`.
- Once the frontier holds at least one answered node and none open or active, an expansive run that forms neither the clean nor the composite candidate gets the hard candidate `frontier_drained`, so a run whose frontier work is done stops instead of running to its cap; a fan-out lineage reads only its own partition. A frontier that closed with nothing answered forms no candidate. Every legal-stop gate and the graph decision still decide it, and a default run never forms it.
- A fan-out lineage keeps its coverage graph in `graph-db/` inside its own directory: the runner exports `DEEP_LOOP_COVERAGE_DB_DIR` to it, so the graph steps stay inside the lineage write surface and the hotspot saturation gate has a graph to read.
- A fan-out run deals the depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order, binds `frontier_scope:` beside `review_focus:`, records the partition as `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json` and returns a partition guard for any node no lineage reports. Size each lineage's iteration cap (the `--iters=N` value on its executor entry, which `fanout-run.cjs` passes as that lineage's `--max-iterations`, 12 when unset) so half of it covers `ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions`.

## 4. RULES
[0m$ [0msed -n '118,132p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md; sed -n '150,162p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md
| MAD noise floor | 0.35 | The latest ratio sits at or below `MAD * 1.4826` |
| Question entropy | 0.35 | Evidence-backed coverage reaches 0.85 |

A normalized score above 0.60 nominates STOP. The legal-stop gates then check that low novelty held for the required rounds, that every key question has an evidence-backed answer, that sources are diverse and that one weak source does not carry a finding. A missing graph decision blocks every non-terminal STOP. When a gate fails, the run records `blocked_stop` and continues with a recovery focus.

The shipped config floors convergence at 3 rounds, sets `stuckThreshold` to 3 and caps a run at 10 rounds and 120 minutes. Lower `--convergence` asks for more rounds. Raise it when the loop should finish sooner.

`--convergence-mode` changes the stop path. `off` disables convergence STOP while the hard caps stay active. `sliding-window` scores novelty over a bounded window. `divergent` sends an eligible legal stop into one bounded pivot round instead of synthesis, then returns to the loop.

`--depth-mode=expansive` lets a run grow its own questions. A seed step writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1, and the leaf may propose children only when its prompt carries a frontier block. An admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, and prunes the proposal on the first failure. Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first and writes it as a frontier line, so visiting a node does not read as answering it. While a node stays open the frontier vetoes a soft convergence stop, which never outranks the iteration cap or the other hard stops, and the caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(10, 2 × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes, 16 at the full six seeds, and the seed step prints the derived budget. The node cap stays half that budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1.

### Stop Reasons

The workflow persists one `stopReason` value when the loop ends or pauses.

| **Rolling average** | 0.30 | Recent severity-weighted finding yield across the last two evidence-bearing iterations |
| **MAD noise floor** | 0.25 | The latest ratio measured against statistical noise |
| **Dimension coverage** | 0.45 | Configured dimensions plus the required traceability protocols are covered, after at least one stabilization pass |

The weighted score has to reach `compositeStopScore`, which defaults to 0.60, before the loop may consider a stop. Clearing the threshold is not enough on its own. Nine binary gates have to pass as well, and a failure writes a `blocked_stop` event with `blockedBy` and a per-gate `gateResults` record. The gate list and its criteria live in `references/convergence/convergence.md`.

Any new P0 forces another iteration. The override sets `newFindingsRatio` to at least 0.50, so a fresh blocker cannot be averaged away. The shipped config adds `antiConvergence.minIterations` of 2 and a `stopPolicy` of `fail-closed`, and the runtime capability loader rejects a missing or permissive stop policy. The floor holds a review run at `CONTINUE` with reason `minIterationsNotReached` until its iteration count reaches `minIterations`, even when every dimension is clean.

`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review. Split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap.

### Fan-out and Merged Verdicts

One run can spread the loop across executor lineages. Pass `--executor` once per lineage, or pass an `--executors` matrix. Cap the pool with `--concurrency`. The merged verdict is strongest restriction: any lineage with an active P0, or a required gate still failing at its stop, turns the merged verdict into FAIL.
> build · deepseek-v4.1-flash
[0m$ [0msed -n '1100,1210p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/fanout-merge.cjs
}
  }

  // Resolved findings are tracked separately per lineage and were previously
  // dropped here, zeroing the merged resolved coverage. Collect them by id with
  // _lineages attribution, without touching open-finding/verdict semantics.
  const resolvedFindingById = mergeOptions.enableNearDuplicateDedup ? createFindingBucketIndex() : new Map();
  for (const { label, registry } of lineageData) {
    if (!registry || !Array.isArray(registry.resolvedFindings)) continue;
    collectUnrankedSeverities(registry.resolvedFindings, label, unrankedSeverities);
    for (const finding of registry.resolvedFindings) {
      const id = finding.findingId || finding.title;
      if (!id) continue;
      // Still active elsewhere — keep it out of the resolved list so it can't appear
      // in both merged lists at once.
      if (activeFindingIds.has(id)) continue;
      if (mergeOptions.enableNearDuplicateDedup) {
        addReviewFinding(getFindingBucket(resolvedFindingById, id, finding, true).records, finding, label, mergeOptions);
      } else if (resolvedFindingById.has(id)) {
        addLineage(resolvedFindingById.get(id), label);
      } else {
        resolvedFindingById.set(id, { ...finding, _lineages: [label] });
      }
    }
  }
  const mergedResolvedFindings = mergeOptions.enableNearDuplicateDedup
    ? flattenFindingBucketIndex(resolvedFindingById, 'findingId', ['findingId', 'title'])
    : sortByContentThenId([...resolvedFindingById.values()], ['findingId', 'title']);

  const mergedFindings = mergeOptions.enableNearDuplicateDedup
    ? flattenFindingBucketIndex(findingById, 'findingId', ['findingId', 'title'])
    : flattenFindingBuckets(findingById, 'findingId', ['findingId', 'title']);
  const activeP0 = mergedFindings.filter((f) => f.severity === 'P0' && (f.disposition ?? f.status) === 'active').length;
  const activeP1 = mergedFindings.filter((f) => f.severity === 'P1' && (f.disposition ?? f.status) === 'active').length;
  const activeP2 = mergedFindings.filter((f) => f.severity === 'P2' && (f.disposition ?? f.status) === 'active').length;

  const failedRequiredGateIds = [...new Set(lineageData.flatMap((lineage) => lineageFailedRequiredGateIds(lineage)))].sort();

  // Both loops number their frontier nodes per lineage and split them the same way, so the
  // review fan-in reuses the same merge and partition guard rather than re-deriving them.
  const hasFrontier = lineageData.some(
    ({ registry }) => registry && registry.frontier && Array.isArray(registry.frontier.nodes),
  );

  // Search debt is one obligation per bug class the search path still owes, so a class
  // two lineages both owe is one debt rather than two; the first lineage's record of it
  // is kept and the union is ordered by that key so arrival order cannot move it.
  const searchDebtByKey = new Map();
  for (const { registry } of lineageData) {
    if (!registry || !Array.isArray(registry.searchDebt)) continue;
    for (const obligation of registry.searchDebt) {
      if (!obligation || typeof obligation !== 'object') continue;
      const key = obligation.bugClass || obligation.id;
      if (!key) continue;
      if (!searchDebtByKey.has(key)) searchDebtByKey.set(key, obligation);
    }
  }
  const mergedSearchDebt = [...searchDebtByKey.keys()].sort().map((key) => searchDebtByKey.get(key));

  // Strongest-restriction verdict
  const { verdict: mergedVerdict } = deriveReviewVerdict({
    activeP0,
    activeP1,
    activeP2,
    openSearchDebt: mergedSearchDebt.length,
    failedRequiredGateIds,
  });

  // Same channel the registry-shape mismatches already use, so an unranked severity reaches
  // both the run's stderr and the merged registry a release decision reads.
  for (const entry of unrankedSeverities) {
    const warning = {
      type: 'schema_mismatch',
      severity: 'warn',
      lineage: entry.lineage,
      findingId: entry.findingId,
      unknownSeverity: entry.severity,
      message: `Finding "${entry.findingId}" from lineage "${entry.lineage}" carries severity "${entry.severity}", which is outside the review severity scale (P0|P1|P2). It is unranked, sorts below every P2, and cannot raise the merged verdict; collapse it to P2 where the finding is rated.`,
    };
    schemaWarnings.push(warning);
    process.stderr.write(JSON.stringify(warning) + '\n');
  }

  return {
    mergedFrom: lineageData.map(({ label }) => label).sort(),
    lineageExecutors: buildLineageExecutors(lineageData),
    mergedVerdict,
    failedRequiredGateIds,
    openFindings: mergedFindings,
    resolvedFindings: mergedResolvedFindings,
    findingsBySeverity: { P0: activeP0, P1: activeP1, P2: activeP2 },
    openFindingsCount: mergedFindings.length,
    resolvedFindingsCount: mergedResolvedFindings.length,
    activeP0,
    activeP1,
    activeP2,
    searchDebt: mergedSearchDebt,
    ...mergeReviewCoverageSurfaces(lineageData),
    ...(schemaWarnings.length > 0 ? { schema_mismatch: schemaWarnings } : {}),
    ...(hasFrontier ? { frontier: mergeResearchFrontier(lineageData, options.frontierPartition) } : {}),
  };
}

### DRV-068 | Cross-mode anti-convergence contract

#### Description
Verify review mode declares the shared anti-convergence floor, fail-closed stop policy, runtime capability validation, and optimizer invariant locks.

#### Scenario Contract
Prompt: `Validate the deep-review anti-convergence floor, fail-closed stop policy, runtime capability resolver, and optimizer invariant guard.`

Expected signals: `deep-review-config.json` has `antiConvergence.minIterations = 2`, `convergenceMode = "default"`, and `stopPolicy = "fail-closed"`; runtime capabilities repeat fail-closed policy; the shared runtime resolver rejects missing or permissive policy; the optimizer manifest locks convergence mode and carries `minIterations<=maxIterations`.

#### Test Execution
> **Feature File:** [DRV-068](../manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md)

### RV-069 | Divergent convergence mode

#### Description

Verify an eligible clean-review STOP pivots into a new read-only direction without changing legal-stop gates, verdict derivation, or target authority.

#### Scenario Contract

Prompt: `Validate the shipped deep-review divergent pivot, synthesis-only verdict mapping, security-gate preservation, and read-only target boundary.`

Expected signals: Only `all_dimensions_clean` is eligible; all nine gates precede the pivot; successful pivots restore `next_dimension` and continue; verdict derivation remains inside `phase_synthesis`; mutation-shaped candidates and fixes are rejected.

#### Test Execution

> **Feature File:** [RV-069](../manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/divergent-convergence-mode.md)

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md-15-## 1. OVERVIEW
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md-16-
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md:17:Documents the cross-mode `antiConvergence` contract as it applies to `deep-review`: a minimum iteration floor, a locked convergence mode, a fail-closed stop policy, runtime capability validation, and optimizer invariants that prevent invalid floor/max-iteration combinations.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md-18-
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md:19:For review mode, the shipped config sets `antiConvergence.minIterations` to `2`, `convergenceMode` to `default`, and `stopPolicy` to `fail-closed`. This makes a one-pass STOP illegal at the config layer and keeps optimizer changes from tuning past the floor contract.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md-20-
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md-21----

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md-23-## 2. HOW IT WORKS
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md-24-
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md:25:The review config declares the floor in `assets/deep-review-config.json`. Its optimizer-managed block marks `antiConvergence.convergenceMode`, `antiConvergence.depthMode` and `antiConvergence.stopPolicy` as locked fields while keeping the review loop's ordinary tunables (`convergenceThreshold`, `stuckThreshold`, `maxIterations`) separate.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md-26-
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md-27-The runtime capability matrix declares `stopPolicy: "fail-closed"`. The shared runtime capability resolver rejects any matrix where `stopPolicy` is absent or not `fail-closed`, so callers cannot silently fall back to permissive stop behavior.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md-28-
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md:29:The optimizer manifest carries an invariant group for the same contract: `antiConvergence.convergenceMode` and `convergenceMode` stay locked, and `antiConvergence.minIterations` is tunable only when the paired `minIterations <= maxIterations` constraint holds.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md-30-
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/severity-system/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md-31----

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/cross-mode-anti-convergence-contract.md:19:Review loops can produce convincing but incomplete early STOP decisions when only one pass has run. title: "Anti-convergence floor"
description: "Blocks convergence STOP until the configured minimum iteration floor clears."
trigger_phrases:
  - "anti-convergence floor"
  - "minIterations convergence"
  - "convergenceMode off"
  - "min_iterations_guard_pass"
  - "premature research stop guard"
version: 1.14.1.0
---

# Anti-convergence floor

## 1. OVERVIEW

Prevents convergence STOP before the configured minimum iteration floor while preserving hard stops and pause behavior.

The anti-convergence floor keeps deep-research from ending after a shallow early signal. Operators get a declarative `minIterations` default and a `convergenceMode` escape hatch without weakening max-iteration, pause, or halt boundaries.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

The config template defines `antiConvergence.minIterations` with a default of `3` and `antiConvergence.convergenceMode` with a default of `default`. The workflow writes those values into the config record, validates older or malformed configs at read time, and clamps the effective floor to the hard `maxIterations` cap when needed.

During convergence checking, a STOP candidate is blocked while the completed iteration count is below the effective floor. When the floor clears, the workflow emits `min_iterations_guard_pass`, keeping attribution separate from quality-guard events. `convergenceMode: "off"` disables convergence-driven STOP while leaving max iterations, pause, and other explicit halts active.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/assets/deep-research-config.json` | Asset | Defines the anti-convergence defaults and locked config fields. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | Workflow | Writes the floor into the config and emits floor events; convergence.cjs enforces the floor before a STOP. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/deep-research-convergence-floor.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Verifies config shape, floor behavior, and backward-compatible missing-field handling. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/convergence-and-recovery/anti-convergence-floor.md` | Manual playbook | Verifies the operator-visible floor and `convergenceMode` contract. |

      const slotWindowStartIso = new Date().toISOString();

      const livenessKey = `${lineage.label}:${attempt}`;

        lineage,
        guards: lineageBudgetGuards,
        maxRetries: fanoutConfig.maxRetries,

      if (!budgetDecision.continue_allowed) {
        appendFanoutStatusLedger(ledgerPath, {
          event: 'budget_cap_exceeded',
          label: lineage.label,
          at: new Date().toISOString(),
          severity: 'error',

          upper_bound: budgetDecision.upper_bound,
          gauges: latestGauges,
        });
        const failure = new Error(
          `lineage ${lineage.label} exceeds configured budget cap: `
            + `${budgetDecision.upper_bound.estimated_cost_units} > ${budgetDecision.upper_bound.max_cost_units_per_lineage} cost units`,
        );
        failure.label = lineage.label;
        failure.exitCode = null;
        failure.timedOut = false;
        failure.budgetCap = budgetDecision;
        throw failure;
      }

[0m$ [0mrg -n --no-config "depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/feature-catalog/convergence/expansive-depth-mode.md; rg -n --no-config "depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle" .skilled/commands/deep/research.md .skilled/commands/deep/review.md
16:`--depth-mode` takes `default` or `expansive`. A run without the flag is unchanged. `expansive` turns on a research frontier: the run seeds depth-one nodes from the topic before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows as nodes under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps, and holds a convergence stop while frontier nodes stay open. Two live research pilots ran on one topic, and both failed the criterion that a stop candidate forms before the iteration cap: each run stopped on `maxIterationsReached`. The criterion was then amended to a frontier drain before the cap, which the second run met: its frontier showed 0 open nodes from the check before iteration 15 of 16. This page describes the shipped code and its tests.
.skilled/commands/deep/review.md:100:| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1 (merges are pruned as `merge-no-own-diff` and index rebuilds as `generated-artifact`), admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths, and a run-wide `maxFrontierNodes` cap that defaults to half of `--max-iterations`), keeps node rows in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl` (the reducer derives answered and budget-exhausted status from them on every reduce), holds open nodes as `searchDebt`, dispatches the active node's next uncovered dimension, turns a review stop candidate into another iteration while nodes stay open, and, once every node is closed and at least one was answered, forms a `frontier_drained` stop candidate that the legal-stop gates still decide, so the run can stop before its cap. A node needs one iteration per dimension in its universe, the four canonical dimensions plus any custom name, and the depth-1 band gets only `budgetSplit.depth1` (0.5) of `--max-iterations`, so set `--max-iterations` to at least `2 * ceil(nodes / lineages) * dimensions` to fund the depth-1 band. It never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, the minIterations floor, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off` or `--stop-policy=max-iterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review; split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap. Fan-out lineages each get a share of the seeded nodes. |
.skilled/commands/deep/research.md:107:| `--depth-mode` | `default` \| `expansive` | `default`, the value when the flag is absent, runs exactly as before. `expansive` is opt-in: the run seeds depth-1 frontier nodes from the topic before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows as new questions under depth, fan-out, budget, dedupe and cycle caps (`expansiveDepth` in the config: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a 50/35/15 iteration budget across depths, and a run-wide `maxFrontierNodes` cap that defaults to half of `--max-iterations`), steers Next Focus to the shallowest open node, and turns a convergence stop into another iteration while frontier nodes stay open. The node cap stays half that budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md:127:`--depth-mode=expansive` lets a run grow its own questions. # Deep Research Quick Reference

Open this when you need the command defaults, the iteration checklist or a fast answer to a convergence question without loading the full protocol.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

### Purpose

Lookup surface during runs covering when to invoke the loop, what each phase produces, how convergence is computed, and where live state lives on disk. Defer to the full protocol and convergence references for deep reasoning.

### When to Use

- Before a run, to confirm the command and its parameter defaults.
- During a run, to follow the iteration checklist and the convergence decision tree.
- After a run, to read the convergence summary and tune or troubleshoot the next one.

### Key Sources

Operator contract source of truth for this page:
- command syntax: `.skilled/commands/deep/research.md`
- convergence stop contract: `references/convergence/convergence.md`, `references/convergence/convergence-signals.md`, and the deep-research YAML workflow
- state packet contract: `references/state/state-format.md`, `references/state/state-jsonl.md`, `references/state/state-outputs.md`, and `references/state/state-reducer-registry.md`

**Provenance:** the fresh-context-per-iteration design is adapted from karpathy/autoresearch (loop concept), AGR (fresh context "Ralph Loop"), pi-autoresearch (JSONL state), and autoresearch-opencode (context injection).

---

## 2. COMMANDS

| Command | Description |
|---------|-------------|
| `/deep:research:auto "topic"` | Run autonomous deep research (no approval gates) |
| `/deep:research:confirm "topic"` | Run with approval gates at each iteration |
| `/deep:research "topic"` | Ask which mode to use |

### Parameters

| Parameter | Default | Description |
|-----------|---------|-------------|
| `--max-iterations` | 10 | Maximum loop iterations |
| `--convergence` | 0.05 | Stop when avg newInfoRatio below this |
| `--depth-mode` | default | `expansive` grows a depth-capped frontier of follow-up questions and vetoes a convergence stop while nodes stay open. A defaulted `--max-iterations` is sized from the seed, up to 24; an explicit one always wins |
| `--spec-folder` | auto | Target spec folder path |
| `progressiveSynthesis` | true | Allow incremental `research/research.md` updates before final synthesis |

---

## 3. WHEN TO USE

| Scenario | Use |
|----------|-----|
| Deep unknown topic, multi-round needed | `/deep:research` |
| Simple question, 1-2 sources | Direct search with `@context` |
| Check prior work only | The continuity ladder, then the ripgrep recipes in `retrieval-conventions.md` |
| Exhaustive critical research | `/deep:research --max-iterations 15 --convergence 0.02` |

---

## 4. ARCHITECTURE

## 5. STATE FILES

| File | Location | Format | Purpose |
|------|----------|--------|---------|
| Config | `research/deep-research-config.json` | JSON | Loop parameters |
| State | `research/deep-research-state.jsonl` | JSONL | Iteration log (append-only) |
| Strategy | `research/deep-research-strategy.md` | Markdown | What worked/failed, next focus |
| Registry | `research/findings-registry.json` | JSON | Reducer-owned open/resolved questions and key findings |
| Dashboard | `research/deep-research-dashboard.md` | Markdown | Reducer-owned lifecycle and convergence summary |
| Iterations | `research/iterations/iteration-NNN.md` | Markdown | Per-iteration findings |
| Output | `research/research.md` | Markdown | Workflow-owned progressive synthesis output |

> **Live lifecycle branches:** `resume`, `restart`, and `completed-continue` through `--extend=N`.
> `fork` is deferred and not runtime-wired. `progressiveSynthesis` defaults to `true`, so `research/research.md` is updated during the loop and finalized at synthesis.

> **Path contract:** root-spec runs write directly to `{spec_folder}/research/`; child-phase and sub-phase first runs use the local `{spec_folder}/research/` directory directly, with `{ownerSlug}-pt-NN` allocated only when prior non-matching content already exists.

> **Canonical pause sentinel:** `research/.deep-research-pause`

> **Runtime capability matrix:** `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/guides/capability-matrix.md` and `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/assets/runtime-capabilities.json`

## 7. LEDGER VOCABULARY AND PROJECTION CEILING

### Registered vocabulary

The canonical dotted stems for this mode live in `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-research-ledger-schema/deep-research-ledger-types.ts`:

- `DeepResearchEventStems` — the registered stems the ledger accepts.
- `DEEP_RESEARCH_STEM_PRODUCERS` — one entry per stem recording whether a mechanical producer emits it today (`spoken`, with the producer files) or why nothing does (`reserved`).

`.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/check-ledger-stem-producers.cjs` scans the workflow assets and runtime scripts and fails (exit 2) when a registered stem has no producer, a spoken stem has no emitter, or a reserved stem is emitted anyway.

### Which file is authoritative

`deep-research-state.jsonl` is authoritative while the mode's authority record (`authority-deep-research.json`, state `legacy_authoritative`) names the legacy writer. Once the record moves to `new_authoritative_reversible` or `new_authoritative_final` with `selectedWriter: "dark"`, the ledger is authoritative and the append gateway refreshes this file as a projection after each append.

### What a projection refresh can reproduce

The projection folds only registered stems, and its `run_initialized` arm rebuilds a thin config row: `type`, `topic`, `maxIterations`, `generation`, `timestamp`. A pre-flip config row written by the workflow carries more — `convergenceThreshold`, `minIterations`, `minIdeaObservations`, `antiConvergence`, `sessionId`, `executor`, `specFolder`, `createdAt` — and a refresh cannot rebuild those keys.

Because losing them silently would look like a complete projection, a replace that would drop keys from an existing `type: "config"` first row is refused with `ATTRIBUTION_COLLAPSE` (invariant `no-attribution-loss-on-replace`) before any bytes are written. An operator who hits it either migrates the attribution into a registered `deep_research.run_initialized` payload or moves the legacy file aside to accept the loss deliberately.

This scenario validates the `--depth-mode` flag and its default. The objective is to confirm that a research run without the flag, or with `--depth-mode=default`, produces the same registry, dashboard, Next Focus, prompt pack and stop config as the loop produced before the expansive mode existed.

### Why This Matters

The expansive mode must stay opt-in. An operator who never passes the flag should see no change in what the loop writes or decides. That promise lets the mode ship without a migration.

An operator who does pass `--depth-mode=expansive` should know its budget rule. Admission caps the live frontier at half of `maxIterations`, and the seed counts toward that cap. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed, `min(24, max(10, 2 × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes, and the seed step prints the derived budget. An explicit `--max-iterations` always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the exact commands below and confirm that the flag documents `default` as its value when absent and that the golden byte-identity suite passes.

- Objective: Confirm that `--depth-mode` defaults to `default`, that `--depth-mode=expansive` is the only opt-in value and that a default run matches the pre-change baseline on every compared artifact.
- Real user request: Run a deep research session without any new flag and tell me whether anything changed for me.
- Prompt: `Check that --depth-mode defaults to default and that a research run without the flag matches the pre-change baseline byte for byte. Name any byte that differs. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Read the flag row and the config default, read the golden helper that removes one paragraph, then run the golden suite by file stem.
- Expected signals: The flag row lists `default` as the value when the flag is absent. The config carries `"depthMode": "default"`. The golden suite passes 12 of 12 tests. The only paragraph the comparison removes is the expansive-only persona paragraph in the CLI lineage prompt.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say a default run matches the baseline byte for byte, except for one expansive-only paragraph in the embedded agent persona of CLI lineage prompts. That exception is a waiver pending operator ratification.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the flag row and the config default both name `default`, the golden suite passes and the helper removes only the persona paragraph; FAIL if any other byte differs or any golden case fails.

The objective is to confirm that a review run without the flag, or with `--depth-mode=default`, produces the same registry, dashboard, Next Focus, prompt pack, pivot seeds, lineage prompt and stop config as the loop produced before the expansive mode existed.

### Why This Matters

The expansive mode must stay opt-in. An operator who never passes the flag should see no change in what the review writes or decides. That promise lets the mode ship without a migration of existing review runs.

An operator who does pass `--depth-mode=expansive` should know its budget rule. Admission caps the live frontier at half of `maxIterations`, and the seed counts toward that cap. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed, `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, or 7 when every seed was pruned, and the seed step prints the derived budget. An explicit `--max-iterations` always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1.

---
title: "Completed-run extension (--extend)"
description: "A completed deep-research run extends in place by N iterations under --extend=N, keeps its session identifier and generation, and records one run_extended event."
trigger_phrases:
  - "--extend"
  - "--extend=N"
  - "extend completed research run"
  - "run_extended"
  - "--extend-minutes"
version: 1.0.2.0
---

# Completed-run extension (--extend)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--extend=N` reopens a completed research run in the same session and generation and adds `N` iterations. The run keeps its `sessionId` and generation, records one `run_extended` ledger event, and keeps the prior synthesis as `research/synthesis-v{generation}-r{continuedFromRun}.md`. `--extend-minutes=M` adds minutes to the duration budget of the same extension.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Lineage And Preconditions

The extension uses lineage mode `completed-continue`. The run must already be complete. An unfinished run continues with `--lineage-mode=resume` instead. The extension is refused with fan-out.

### Iteration And Duration Budget

The iteration cap rises by `N` over the prior count. `--extend-minutes=M` is valid only with `--extend`. The new duration budget is `max(prior, elapsed) + M`, because the clock counts time since `createdAt`, so an extension started after the old budget still runs. Without `--extend-minutes`, `M` is the prior budget, and a run with no duration budget keeps none.

### Stop Interaction

Under `stopPolicy: convergence`, an extension with nothing new can stop again at once on `all_questions_answered`. Pass `--convergence-mode=off`, use `stopPolicy: max-iterations`, or add questions to `research/inbox.jsonl`.

### Ledger And Reducer Routing

The `run_extended` stem is part of the deep-research ledger schema, and the deep-research reducer routes it. The lifecycle event filters in `deep-research/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` read the same event.

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

| File | Layer | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/commands/deep/research.md` | Command doc | Flag rows for `--extend` and `--extend-minutes`. |
| `.skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-research.yaml` | Command workflow | Lineage handling and the snapshot of the prior synthesis. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-research-ledger-schema/deep-research-ledger-schema.ts` | Shared | Defines the `run_extended` stem and its payload. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/lib/deep-research-reducers/deep-research-reducer.ts` | Shared | Routes the `run_extended` stem. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/scripts/reduce-state.cjs` | Script | Lifecycle event filters that read the extension event. |

### Validation And Tests

| File | Type | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/tests/unit/deep-research-reducers.vitest.ts` | Vitest | Reducer routing for the run lifecycle event stems. |
| `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/manual-testing-playbook/entry-points-and-modes/extend-completed-research-run.md` | Manual playbook | DR-065: same-session continuation, the `run_extended` event and the synthesis snapshot. |

---
title: "Completed-run extension flags (--extend, --extend-minutes)"
description: "The --extend=N flag on /deep:research and /deep:review reopens a completed single-executor run in place, and --extend-minutes=M, valid only with --extend, adds minutes to that run's duration budget. Neither flag changes the registry-driven workflowMode, runtimeLoopType and backendKind classification."
trigger_phrases:
  - "--extend"
  - "--extend-minutes"
  - "completed run extension flag"
  - "extend a completed deep loop run"

# Completed-run extension flags (--extend, --extend-minutes)

## 1. OVERVIEW

`--extend=N` and `--extend-minutes=M` are command-level options on `/deep:research` and `/deep:review`. They reopen a completed run in place. The flags are not part of `mode-registry.json`, so the hub classifies a request into the same workflow mode with or without them. The extension behavior lives in the research and review packets.

---

## 2. HOW IT WORKS

### Hub Classification Unchanged

`mode-registry.json` carries the `workflowMode`, `runtimeLoopType` and `backendKind` discriminator for `research` and `review`, and it has no extension field. Routing resolves the same packet whether the flags are present or not.

### Flag Routing

The command workflow binds `--extend=N` as `extend` with lineage mode `completed-continue`, and `--extend-minutes=M` as `extend_minutes`. Both take positive integers, and `--extend-minutes` is valid only with `--extend`. The extension step sets `maxIterations` to the iterations run so far plus N and keeps the session and generation. A config with fan-out executors is refused before any lineage spawns.

### Packet Behavior

The extension behavior is documented in the packet catalogs: [completed-run extension for research](../../deep-research/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/completed-run-extension.md) and [completed-run extension for review](../../deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/completed-run-extension.md).

---

## 3. SOURCE FILES

### Implementation

# DRV-080 -- Frontier open veto holds a review stop, hard stops included

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-080`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the `frontierOpen` veto in the review stop decision for `DRV-080`. The objective is to confirm that a review stop waits while frontier nodes stay open, that the veto covers the clean stop that counts as a hard stop, and that every hard limit keeps its precedence.

### Why This Matters

A review that has checked every configured dimension looks finished. The veto keeps the review going while a sub-target is still open. It must never outrank a hard limit, or a review could loop past its budget. It must also cover the clean stop, or the veto would hold back only the soft stops.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run the commands below and confirm the review veto table and the guard line that makes review candidates vetoable.

- Objective: Confirm that an open node vetoes a review stop candidate only under the expansive mode, that the veto covers `all_dimensions_clean`, which is a hard stop, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy keep their own decisions.
- Real user request: The review said it was clean, but it kept going. Why, and do the hard limits still stop it on time?
- Prompt: `Check that under expansive depth mode a review stop candidate continues while frontier nodes are open, including the clean stop, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy still decide first. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`
- Expected execution process: Run the review veto table and the composite case by file stem, read the guard line that marks review candidates vetoable, then read the veto branch that sits after the floor check.
- Expected signals: All eight table rows pass. The row for open nodes returns `CONTINUE` with `frontierOpen` for the clean stop. The row for an empty frontier returns `STOP` with `all_dimensions_clean`. The cap, floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and max-iterations rows keep their own decisions. The composite case returns `CONTINUE` with `frontierOpen` while nodes are open and `STOP` with `composite_converged` once they drain. The guard line makes every review candidate vetoable.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The operator can say a review waits for its open sub-targets, clean stop included, and the hard limits still stop the run on time.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the table, the composite case and the guard line all pass or print as described; FAIL if an open node lets a review stop through, or a hard limit loses to the veto.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prerequisites

- Working directory is the repository root.
- The `runtime/` vitest suites are installed under `.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime`.

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Check that under expansive depth mode a review stop candidate continues while frontier nodes are open, including the clean stop, and that the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy still decide first. Return a PASS or FAIL verdict with the reason.`

### Commands

1. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run stop-decision.vitest -t "expansive review candidate|vetoes a composite review stop while the frontier is open" --reporter=verbose`
2. `rg -n -F "loopType === 'review' || !candidate.hardStop" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs`

### Expected

Command 1 prints `Tests  9 passed` (the rest of the file skipped). The eight table rows are named `expansive review candidate: <row label>`, and the composite case is `vetoes a composite review stop while the frontier is open`. The row `open nodes veto the clean stop` returns `CONTINUE` with `frontierOpen`, and the row `an empty frontier lets the clean stop through` returns `STOP` with `all_dimensions_clean`. Command 2 prints one line, the guard that sets `vetoable` to true for every review loop and for any research candidate that is not a hard stop.

The table covers the cap, the floor, stuck recovery, convergence off and the max-iterations policy. It does not cover consecutive failures or pause, so this scenario makes no claim about those two.

### Evidence

- The verbose case lines and summary line from command 1, with its exit status.
- The line from command 2, read from the current `convergence.cjs`.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: Command 1 exits 0 with its nine cases passing, and command 2 prints the guard line.
- **Fail**: An open node lets a review stop through, the clean stop escapes the veto, a hard limit loses to the veto, or command 2 prints nothing.

### Failure Triage

1. An open node lets a review stop through: check that the veto reads the expansive flag and the count of open or active nodes from the review facts.
2. The clean stop escapes the veto: check the `vetoable` guard that command 2 prints. A review loop must stay vetoable whatever the candidate's `hardStop` value.
3. A hard limit loses to the veto: check the order of the veto and the cap, floor and stuck checks in the decision, since the veto must come after the floor.

---

| `stop-decision.vitest` | The review veto table, the composite case and the empty-frontier case |

title: "DRV-072 -- Review run holds below its minIterations floor"

version: 1.0.2.0

# DRV-072 -- Review run holds below its minIterations floor

This document captures the realistic user-testing contract, current behavior, execution flow, source anchors, and metadata for `DRV-072`.

---

## 1. OVERVIEW

This scenario validates the minimum-iteration floor for `DRV-072`. It focuses on a review run whose stop candidate arrives before its configured `minIterations`: the convergence check must return `CONTINUE` with reason `minIterationsNotReached`, and the run may release only once the floor is reached.

### Why This Matters

The floor used to apply to research runs only. A review that reached a stop candidate at iteration 2 with a floor of 3 could stop one iteration early, before the reviewer had the second full pass the floor exists to guarantee. The shipped floor of 2 is unchanged, so only an operator who sets a higher floor sees the change.

---

## 2. SCENARIO CONTRACT

Operators run this as a single check of the convergence decision with a floor set above the stop candidate, so the outcome comes from the floor and not from the default.

- Objective: Confirm that a review stop candidate below `minIterations` returns `CONTINUE` with `minIterationsNotReached`, and that the same run releases at the floor.
- Real user request: I set a review to run at least three iterations. Do not let it stop at two, even if it looks converged.
- Prompt: `Stop a deep-review run that reaches a stop candidate at iteration 2 with minIterations set to 3, and confirm the run continues with minIterationsNotReached and releases only at iteration 3.`
- Expected execution process: Read the floor check in the convergence script, read the stop-order item in the review workflow, then run the convergence and stop-decision tests that drive a real review loop state through the floor.
- Expected signals: The floor check returns `CONTINUE` with reason `minIterationsNotReached` at iteration 2, the same loop state releases at iteration 3, the shipped floor of 2 still passes its replay, the review workflow names the floor as stop-order item `4a`, and the stop-decision case reads that a review run gets the floor.
- Desired user-visible outcome: The user is told the review will not stop before its configured minimum, and why it continued.
- Pass/fail: PASS if the iteration-2 candidate continues with `minIterationsNotReached`, the iteration-3 candidate releases, and the replay and parity suites pass; FAIL if a review releases below its floor or the floor applies to research only.

---

## 3. TEST EXECUTION

### Prompt

- Prompt: `Stop a deep-review run that reaches a stop candidate at iteration 2 with minIterations set to 3, and confirm the run continues with minIterationsNotReached and releases only at iteration 3.`

### Commands

1. `rg -n 'minIterationsNotReached' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs`
2. `rg -n '4a\. if iteration_count' .skilled/commands/deep/assets/deep-review.yaml`
3. `cd .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/runtime && npx vitest run review-depth-convergence.vitest stop-decision.vitest review-stop-path-replay.vitest stop-policy-yaml-parity.vitest`

### Expected

Command 1 prints the floor's `CONTINUE` return with `minIterationsNotReached`. Command 2 prints stop-order item `4a`. Command 3 exits 0 with no failed test. The `review-depth-convergence` case shows `CONTINUE` at iteration 2 and a release at iteration 3 from the same loop state.

### Evidence

Capture the command 1 and 2 matches, and the command 3 summary with the named cases.

### Pass / Fail

- **Pass**: The iteration-2 candidate returns `CONTINUE` with `minIterationsNotReached`, the iteration-3 candidate releases, the replay still passes at the shipped floor, and the YAML parity suite passes.
- **Fail**: A review releases below its floor, the reason is missing or wrong, the replay fails at the shipped floor, or the stop-order item is absent from the workflow.

### Failure Triage

If the run releases at iteration 2, check that the floor check in `convergence.cjs` runs on the candidate before the legal-stop gates and no longer depends on the loop type. If the shipped replay fails, the floor value has changed, since the shipped floor of 2 must stay as it is.

### Optional Supplemental Checks

- Shipped floor: run the stop-path replay with its fixtures unchanged and confirm the outcome matches its recorded expectation.

[0m$ [0mrg -n --no-config "the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on" .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/SKILL.md .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/convergence/convergence.md .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review. Split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/convergence/convergence.md:105:`--depth-mode=expansive` grows a depth-capped frontier of sub-target nodes beside the review target. The value lands in `antiConvergence.depthMode`, and the caps live in `expansiveDepth`: `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `budgetSplit` of 0.5/0.35/0.15 across the depth bands, and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations` unless the config sets a number. Once a band's spend reaches its `budgetSplit` share of `maxIterations`, the reducer closes every open or active node in that band as `exhausted` with reason `budget`, and an unspent share rolls down to the next band only after its own band holds no open node. The run cap never prunes a depth-1 seed, which only the root-children cap bounds, but every seed that is not pruned, answered ones included, counts toward the live total that later admissions are checked against. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. A fan-out parent seeds before any config exists, so its cap falls back to 10. The mode is locked against the optimizer; the `expansiveDepth` block is in neither its tunable nor its locked list. An absent flag means `default`, which runs exactly as before.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/state/state-format.md:472:5. When any rule fails, the workflow appends `{"event":"claim_adjudication","passed":false,"missingPackets":[...]}` to the state log. The next `step_check_convergence` call reads that event and sets `claimAdjudicationGate` = `false`, producing a `blockedStop` event with `blockedBy: ["claimAdjudicationGate"]` until a follow-up iteration rewrites the packet.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:192:If convergence math or a hard-stop candidate points to STOP, `runtime/scripts/convergence.cjs` runs the review legal-stop decision tree before the loop stops. That decision tree records nine review-specific gates: `convergenceGate`, `dimensionCoverageGate`, `p0ResolutionGate`, `evidenceDensityGate`, `hotspotSaturationGate`, `claimAdjudicationGate`, `fixCompletenessReplayGate`, `candidateCoverageGate`, and `graphlessFallbackGate` (see `../convergence/convergence.md` §1 blocked_stop event example for the authoritative shape). The Step 4a claim-adjudication path treats `claimAdjudicationGate` as a hard STOP gate, so the count covers both the convergence decision tree and the adjudication enforcement path. `candidateCoverageGate` and `graphlessFallbackGate` are v2-rollout gates that pass trivially when the review-depth-v2 search path is inactive. If any gate fails, the loop does **not** stop. Instead it emits a first-class `blocked_stop` JSONL event with:
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:194:- `blockedBy`: array of the failed review gate names
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:195:- `gateResults`: per-gate pass/fail payloads using the review gate names above
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:395:**Failure semantics**: when any new P0/P1 finding is missing a packet or a required field, the workflow records `{"event":"claim_adjudication","passed":false,"missingPackets":[...]}` in `deep-review-state.jsonl`. On the next loop, `step_check_convergence` step 0 (universal pre-check) routes STOP to `BLOCKED` with `blockedBy: ["claimAdjudicationGate"]` until a follow-up iteration rewrites the packet. Downgraded findings have their `finalSeverity` updated. The original severity is preserved in the iteration file for audit trail.

[0m$ [0msed -n '124,142p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md; sed -n '100,118p' .skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md
### State on Disk

The packet lives under `{spec_folder}/review/`. A root-spec target writes there directly. A child-phase target writes flat on its first run and gets a `pt-NN` sibling packet only when prior content for a different target already exists.

| File under `{spec_folder}/review/` | Role |
|---|---|
| `deep-review-config.json` | Run settings. Immutable after init, apart from the terminal `status: complete` flip and the rewrite an `--extend=N` extension makes |
| `deep-review-state.jsonl` | Append-only log of iterations, events and stop decisions |
| `deep-review-findings-registry.json` | Reducer-owned index of every finding and its state |
| `deep-review-strategy.md` | Dimension coverage, findings and the next audit focus |
| `deep-review-dashboard.md` | Reducer-generated convergence trend |
| `.deep-review-pause` | Operator-owned pause sentinel |
| `resource-map.md` | Emitted at synthesis unless the run passes `--no-resource-map` |
| `review-report.md` | The nine-section report, written at synthesis |
| `iterations/iteration-NNN.md` | One write-once file per iteration |

The lineage block carries `sessionId`, `parentSessionId`, `lineageMode`, `generation`, `continuedFromRun` and `releaseReadinessState`. A first run is mode `new`. `resume` continues the same lineage without changing generation. `restart` opens a new generation and archives the prior packet. `completed-continue` is set by `--extend=N`, which reopens a completed run in the same session and generation and records `continuedFromRun`.
`fork` is reserved and deferred, so the runtime neither emits nor accepts it. `releaseReadinessState` starts at `in-progress`. It moves to `release-blocking` while any unresolved P0 remains. It moves to `converged` when the run stops converged with a PASS or CONDITIONAL verdict.
| `deep-research-strategy.md` | Reducer | Key questions, next focus, what worked and what failed |
| `findings-registry.json` | Reducer | Question state, key findings, ruled-out directions and blocked-stop history |
| `deep-research-dashboard.md` | Reducer | Iteration table, trend, dead ends and active risks |
| `iterations/iteration-NNN.md` | Agent | One round of cited findings |
| `deltas/iter-NNN.jsonl` | Agent | The structured delta for that round |
| `research.md` | Workflow | The canonical synthesis of the run |
| `resource-map.md` | Workflow | The optional evidence-derived map of cited files |
| `.deep-research-pause` | Operator | The pause sentinel checked before each dispatch |

The workflow owns the config and the synthesis. The agent writes its iteration file and delta, plus any progressive synthesis contribution the config allows. The reducer owns the strategy sections and the registry. It redraws the dashboard. Because every artifact is on disk, a crashed run resumes from the packet files.

### Convergence

A low novelty score only nominates STOP.

| Signal | Weight | Votes STOP when |
|---|---|---|
| Rolling average | 0.30 | The last three ratios average below `convergenceThreshold` |
| MAD noise floor | 0.35 | The latest ratio sits at or below `MAD * 1.4826` |

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. A seed step writes the depth-1 sub-target nodes before iteration 1, the active node's next uncovered dimension steers the iteration, and an admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, pruning the proposal on the first failure. Node status lives in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, which no default run creates, and an open node is filed as open search debt, so the run spends the iteration that node still needs. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review. Split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap.

### The Packet Files

Every run keeps continuity under `{spec_folder}/research/`. A root spec writes there directly. A child-phase target writes flat on a first run and adds a `{basename(spec_folder)}-pt-{NN}` folder only when prior content exists for a different target.

| File | Owner | What it holds |
|---|---|---|
| `deep-research-config.json` | Workflow | Loop parameters and lineage. Read-only after init |
| `deep-research-state.jsonl` | Append gateway | Config record, iteration records and typed events |
| `deep-research-strategy.md` | Reducer | Key questions, next focus, what worked and what failed |
| `findings-registry.json` | Reducer | Question state, key findings, ruled-out directions and blocked-stop history |
| `deep-research-dashboard.md` | Reducer | Iteration table, trend, dead ends and active risks |
| `iterations/iteration-NNN.md` | Agent | One round of cited findings |
| `deltas/iter-NNN.jsonl` | Agent | The structured delta for that round |
| `research.md` | Workflow | The canonical synthesis of the run |
| `resource-map.md` | Workflow | The optional evidence-derived map of cited files |
| `.deep-research-pause` | Operator | The pause sentinel checked before each dispatch |

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/README.md:127:`--depth-mode=expansive` lets a run grow its own questions. A seed step writes 3 to 6 depth-1 nodes before iteration 1, and the leaf may propose children only when its prompt carries a frontier block. An admission step before the reducer applies six checks in order, relevance, dedupe, depth, fan-out, budget and cycle, and prunes the proposal on the first failure. Next Focus reads the shallowest open node first and writes it as a frontier line, so visiting a node does not read as answering it. While a node stays open the frontier vetoes a soft convergence stop, which never outranks the iteration cap or the other hard stops, and the caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(10, 2 × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes, 16 at the full six seeds, and the seed step prints the derived budget. The node cap stays half that budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1.

.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md:3:description: "Opt-in --depth-mode=expansive on deep-review seeds depth-one sub-target nodes from the review target, keeps node status in review/frontier-nodes.jsonl, and holds a review stop while nodes stay open."
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/feature-catalog/loop-lifecycle/expansive-depth-mode.md:16:`--depth-mode` takes `default` or `expansive`. A review without the flag is unchanged. `expansive` seeds depth-one sub-target nodes from the review target before iteration 1, admits leaf `frontier_proposal` rows under the same caps as research, keeps node status in `review/frontier-nodes.jsonl`, and holds open nodes as `searchDebt`. Two live review pilots ran. In the first the depth-1 budget check passed but the stop criterion failed: the root fan-out cap of 6, since raised to 8 for a review seed, pruned a seventh depth-1 node, and three of six lineages stopped on `maxIterationsReached`. The second, at `maxIterations` 16, seeded eight depth-1 nodes and answered all eight, and the merged frontier ended with 0 open and 0 active nodes under a passing partition guard, which met the amended pilot criterion. This page describes the shipped code and its tests.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-review/README.md:158:`--depth-mode=expansive` is the one stop-path change that grows work instead of ending it. The frontier veto never outranks the iteration cap, consecutive failures, stuck recovery, pause, `--convergence-mode=off`, `--stop-policy=max-iterations` or the floor above, and stuck recovery closes the active node as `exhausted`. Once every node is closed and at least one was answered, a run that forms no other stop candidate gets `frontier_drained`, which the legal-stop gates still decide, so a run whose frontier work is done stops before its cap. The caps are `maxDepth` 3, `maxChildrenPerNode` 6, a `0.5/0.35/0.15` budget split and `maxFrontierNodes`, which defaults to half of `maxIterations`. When `--max-iterations` is left at its default, the run sizes its budget from the seed: `min(24, max(7, 2 × D × (S + maxDepth − 1)))` iterations for S seeded depth-1 nodes and D configured dimensions, and the seed step prints the derived budget. With the four canonical dimensions the ceiling of 24 binds from two seeds on, so one run cannot fund every node at every depth of a wide review. Split a wide review with fan-out, where each lineage sizes its own budget from its share of the seed. A lineage dealt no seed node, or a run whose seeds were all pruned, runs at its loop default instead: 12 iterations for a native lineage and 7 for a CLI one or a single run. The node cap stays half the budget, which leaves room for at least two nodes past depth 1. An explicit `--max-iterations`, marker or fan-out matrix `iterations` value always wins, even below the derived value, and the seed warns when it leaves no room past depth 1. At about seven to eight minutes an iteration, a 24-iteration review can reach `maxDurationMinutes` (120) before its iteration cap.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/state/state-format.md:81:`research/frontier-nodes.jsonl` is the node-status store for a run started with `--depth-mode=expansive`. A default run never creates it. Rows are append-only and carry `{nodeId, parentId, depth, scope, question, status, reason, iteration, dedupeKey}`. The last row for a node wins, so a status change is one more row rather than an edit. `runtime/scripts/frontier-admit.cjs` is the only writer.
.skilled/skills/system-deep-loop/deep-research/references/protocol/loop-protocol.md:421:On a fan-out run, `fanout-run.cjs --depth-mode expansive` deals the seeded open depth-1 nodes round-robin in node-id order across lineages, writes each lineage's `frontier-nodes.jsonl` and inbox rows before it spawns, binds `frontier_scope:` in the lineage prompt and records `frontier_partition` in `orchestration-summary.json`. `fanout-merge.cjs` merges the lineage frontiers, each node keeping its lineage label, and records a partition guard that names any dealt node no lineage reports.

The deep-research loop persists continuity in packet files so each iteration can run with fresh context.

| File | Format | Purpose | Mutability |
|------|--------|---------|------------|
| `deep-research-config.json` | JSON | Loop parameters and lineage | Created at init; read-only after |
| `deep-research-state.jsonl` | JSONL | Append-only structured log | Append-only |
| `deep-research-strategy.md` | Markdown | Current research plan and next focus | Reducer-managed sections |
| `deep-research-findings-registry.json` / `findings-registry.json` | JSON | Reducer-owned findings and question state | Auto-generated |
| `deep-research-dashboard.md` | Markdown | Operator summary | Auto-generated |
| `iterations/iteration-NNN.md` | Markdown | Per-iteration narrative | Write-once |
| `deltas/iter-NNN.jsonl` | JSONL | Per-iteration structured delta | Write-once |
| `research.md` | Markdown | Final/progressive synthesis | Workflow-owned |
| `resource-map.md` | Markdown | Optional evidence-derived resource map | Workflow-owned |

The artifact directory is resolved by `resolveArtifactRoot(specFolder, 'research')` from `.skilled/skills/system-spec-kit/shared/review-research-paths.cjs`.

---

## 2. PACKET LOCATION

Root specs use:

```text
{spec_folder}/research/
```

Child-phase and sub-phase targets use flat-first behavior:

- first run with an empty local `research/` directory writes flat at `{spec_folder}/research/`;
- a `{basename(spec_folder)}-pt-NN` subfolder is allocated only when prior content already exists for a non-matching target;
- continuation runs reuse the existing matching packet.

This avoids an unnecessary `pt-01` wrapper on first runs.

**Example (first run on a child phase):** `.../026-graph.../019-system-hardening/001-initial-research/004-desc-regen/` writes to `004-desc-regen/research/` directly.
